mr. chairman, please! the single market is a project worth fighting for. so we should not only praise his accomplishments but also see his shortcomings and work on them. the union market is becoming less competitive with china or even the friendly united states. escaping the increasingly burdensome regulations that are being imposed on other countries will not level the playing field for our entrepreneurs. the commission must address neglected areas of trade such as services and the barriers that still block our economic potential. this is the only way to make our economy more competitive. i call on the commission to make it its aim once again to complete the single market in services with all its freedoms. i also call on the commission to commit itself to a policy that is sensitive to the poorer areas of the common market in the face of growing interregional disparities, so that no region of the european union is left behind. that is my wish for the single market on its 30th birthday.
in writing. the pandora papers revelations over a year ago revealed the ways in which tax structures using front companies operate. they also revealed the people who use these structures. wealthy individuals have skillfully hidden their wealth under the umbrella of 'paper' companies located in tax havens. such actions result in a loss of revenue of eur 20 billion per year for the member states. that is why we urgently need to regulate the activities of such companies. the point is not to ban innovative business practices, but to level the playing field by denying tax benefits to companies that abuse them to avoid paying income tax on wealthy individuals. my country, poland, has been calling for a fair and united tax system in europe for years. a tax system that respects the fiscal sovereignty of the member states but also allows for the flexible shaping of fiscal policy, which is an important pillar of economic policy. the european parliament's report is very reasonable and balanced on this point. it explains precisely the consequences of considering an entity to be a front, and proposes a number of criteria, such as the share of so-called passive income in the total income, which will enable the national authorities to determine the real purpose of the undertaking and to respond to abuses. i hope that this regulation, which is sensitive to some eu members, will be finalised quickly in the council and that the much needed transparency legislation can be put into effect.

member of the commission. – mr president, i thank the honourable members, and let me also join the warm welcome to the group of petitioners from spain who are present here. today we are discussing a motion for a resolution on the creation of a european capital of local trade and retail, and this proposal is built upon a petition put forward by federations of small retailers keen to give a more prominent role to local trade and small retail. the petition committee was unanimous to support this petition, and the commission also sees value added in this initiative. first, let me emphasise the economic importance of the retail industrial ecosystem. it represents 11.5% of eu value added. it directly employs more than 29 million people across europe, and it’s key for our citizens. consumers spend on average 30% of their budget for their shopping through retail. and of course, the sme dimension is crucial: 5.5 million companies are active in the ecosystem, 99% of which are smes. and we know how the retail ecosystem was badly hurt by the covid-19 pandemic. shops were either closed or bound by stringent precautionary measures. thanks to a strong and ambitious eu response we managed to alleviate many of these impacts, but still smes were the ones which suffered most. so today the energy crisis is also harsh on retailers and in particular on retail smes. we all have in mind the bakeries example, here again we have worked hard to provide emergency solutions through energy policy or state-aid tools. honourable members, retailers play a crucial role in our communities. they provide local employment, proximity to consumers and build the social fabric. a thriving small retail sector is essential for attracting city centres. retail is equally important for maintaining lively rural areas and boosting local supply chains, and through its ‘revitalise retail’ actions, the commission is supporting these objectives. back in 2018, the commission published a guide for competent authorities to help them support their local small retailers to modernise and revitalise. based on the guide, the commission has organised workshops to encourage sharing best practices. during the covid-19 pandemic, we held virtual worship workshops with a focus on sharing innovative ways to keep in touch with consumers. and currently we are gathering stories of retail smes who have successfully embraced innovative green and digital solutions and transformed their business models. they should provide inspiration for smes wishing to respond to the challenges of digital and green transition. last but not least, i would like to mention the transition pathways announced in the industrial strategy update. such pathways support the green and digital transition of industrial ecosystems, among which the retail ecosystem. the commission is planning the launch of a public consultation on it in the coming months. but it may be not enough. in this context, the commission considers that the proposal to create a european capital of local trade and small retail can take the revitalise retail action even further. beyond that, the draft resolution also includes several additional proposed measures, which confirms a lot of enthusiasm, and many ideas to support the small retail sector. and we will assess further how to benefit and how to best follow up on these measures while avoiding duplications. and on the central issue of the creation of the european capital of local trade, the commission would welcome if the european parliament would support the action through a pilot project, and i’m convinced it would be a very positive development in the long-standing commission and the european parliament commitment to support smes and in particular retail smes.

member of the commission. thank you very much for asking. first of all, it should be emphasized that the higher food price increases than inflation are due to the fact that food production has been more affected by the increase in costs. because while the rise in energy or fuel costs - and in general all energy sources - has affected the whole economy, agriculture has also been hit by the high cost of fertilisers, which are linked to energy costs. that's why this food inflation is so visible above the general level of inflation. as far as fertilisers are concerned, the commission has proposed everything within its powers, within its power. we've offered all the instruments we can. most of them depend on the will and decision of the member states, for example whether or not they support their fertiliser producers. i know that there are plans to provide such aid in poland, for example. aid to producers should lead to a reduction in fertiliser prices, followed by a reduction in production costs and a reduction in prices. but you have to remember that not all of the costs are dependent on the farmers, and i would even say that the cost of food is less dependent on the farmers' share of the price. we see that even this increase in agricultural income of about 13 percent doesn't match the increase in food prices, which is over 18 percent. so farmers are not the beneficiaries of this increase. some people try to interpret this as a price increase, but farmers are getting more for their produce and things are generally better. unfortunately, no. agricultural income has not increased enough to offset the increased costs of production. that's why farmers are in a tough spot. i hope that the new strategic plans contained in the cap and a certain flexibility in those plans will help to overcome these difficulties.

member of the commission, on behalf of the vice-president of the commission / high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. – mr president, honourable members, let me begin by thanking the rapporteur, ms isabel wiseler-lima, the shadow rapporteurs and all the members who have contributed to this annual report on human rights and democracy. the human rights situation is worsening across the world. your report, looking back at the last 12 months, takes note of this clearly. we have seen the worst violation of human rights in ukraine, afghanistan, tigray and iran, to name just the most recent and dramatic examples. the universality of human rights are increasingly questioned. some countries are pushing new alternative narratives to change agreed international human rights law and to erode the fundamental rights that belong to every human being. this is something which we cannot accept. human rights are not a matter of choice, but are the basis of human dignity. they are also in the dna of the european union and lie at the foundation of all relations with the rest of the world and they are a core priority for eu foreign policy. we will condemn, systematically, human rights violations and apply sanctions against the worst offenders. the new eu global human rights sanctions regime that we created in 2021, as requested by this house, allows us to go after violations wherever they occur. in december 2022 the 24th eu-ngo forum gathered more than 230 human rights defenders from over 70 countries to discuss how to stop impunity and build a road to accountability and justice. this will remain one of our key priorities in 2023. we are setting up a new global observatory on the fight against impunity with a budget of around eur 20 million for up to five years to gather information and build a knowledge-base on genocide, crimes against humanity and other serious human rights violations. in 2023, we will support the international criminal court via our co-funding of eur 7 million for 21 to 27 and the eu exceptional assistance measure to the office of the prosecutor, karim khan, prosecutor of the icc, eur 7.25 million for 18 months, adopted in june 2022 on vicious war crimes in ukraine. finally, at the multilateral level, we will build on what we achieved in 2022. like in the cases of belarus, ukraine, afghanistan and myanmar, the rights of the child and freedom of religion and belief, and will continue to defend international human rights and humanitarian law, supporting strong resolutions addressing grave human rights situations. we will continue using all our bilateral channels to protect and support those that defend human rights and democracy on the ground. since 2015 the eu human rights defenders mechanism has supported more than 58 000 human rights defenders and their families in more than 120 countries, through eur 35 million of eu funding. as an example, a special support has been mobilised for 300 afghan defenders. since january 2022, we helped at least 3630 individuals at risk. this is never enough, we know. to further step up our support we have recently renewed our commitment to fund our human rights defenders mechanism with eur 30 million for 2022-2027. we also need to make the private sector more involved on businesses, trade and human rights. in 2022, the commission proposed draft legislation on corporate sustainability due diligence and on banning forced labour products from eu markets. we hope to adopt these legislations this year. countering racial and other forms of discrimination, defending gender equality and women’s and girls’ rights is and will stay at the core of our action. other concrete areas for work in 2023 include digital transformation and surveillance technologies, the human rights consequence of climate change and environmental degradation and the right to development. honourable members, in 2023 the international community, and we as the european union, celebrate the 75th anniversary of the universal declaration of human rights. we are already at work with the united nations, partners across the world, as well as civil society and others. may this anniversary be used to join our forces and reverse the current global trend. we have to make sure that 2023 is a year of action and results for human rights. thank you for your long-standing and principled support.
rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on women's rights and gender equality. – mr president, women’s rights are human rights; in 2023, it cannot be anymore an empty slogan, but it’s still a case. millions of girls and women and lgbti+ people around the world continue to experience everyday discrimination in the enjoyment of civil, cultural, economic, political and social rights. in every conflict and every crisis, girls and women always pay the highest price. they are on the front of the victims and they are often even used as a weapons of war. fighting human rights globally, we need also to be credible, commissioner. we live in a europe of inequalities where women in poland have less rights than in 2004 when poland was entering eu. where within european union we have lgbti+ zones. so while talking about human rights worldwide, we should think also about our own credibility. and it’s always the case.
i'm not going to let you get away with this. it is good that after a few months of appeals in this room for the establishment of such an international tribunal, we actually agree that it should be established. there's just a question of pace and time. and today we call for it to be built as soon as possible, as soon as possible to start work, because ukraine not only needs weapons to win and save its homeland. ukraine needs financial help, that's obvious. but there is also a need for hope of justice, for no crime can go unpunished, no criminal can go unpunished. and let's not say that this is a special tribunal, that we're looking for a legal formula. a dozen or so similar international criminal tribunals have already been established. we know what the legal basis is. there were tribunals for the criminals of the former yugoslavia, lebanon, rwanda, sierra leone, and the nuremberg tribunal. let's move quickly and launch this tribunal immediately, because it is an important signal to our security on the continent, and also to putin that his time is running out.

mr. chairman, please! poland welcomes the efforts of the commission and the european external action service to develop a comprehensive european union connectivity strategy, which have resulted in the global gateway. the global gateway is not just a strategy for the european union to provide development aid. it is a comprehensive offer to deliver strategic infrastructure investments in five key areas: digitalisation, transport, energy and climate, health and education. this is a new approach, requiring a wide range of competences beyond development aid. the implementation of this strategy requires a different area of expertise, mainly related to the planning, preparation, financing and implementation of infrastructure investments and permanent, close cooperation with the private sector. it is important to emphasise the importance of working with small and medium-sized enterprises. at the same time, the global gateway can be key to promoting the union's strategic interests globally. it is therefore a key instrument for the european union's foreign policy and position building and for influencing the direction of socioeconomic development in the world. with regard to the key elements of the global gateway strategy, poland draws attention in particular to the need to give priority to the european union's closest neighbourhood, namely the eastern partnership, the western balkans, central asia and the southern neighbourhood, as areas of particular importance from the point of view of the union's strategic interests, in the implementation of the global gateway. the economic and investment plans for these regions should be flagship initiatives of the global gateway. the three seas initiative together with the investment fund can play an important role in achieving the objectives in this area, in particular the eastern partnership and the western balkans.

madam president, we must continue to help ukraine at this crucial moment, including by increasing arms shipments. on the other hand, we should get virtually the entire international community to join in condemning russian aggression. there are still many countries which, inexplicably, abstain or adopt very unreasonable positions of neutrality. the condemnation should not be merely declarative, but should be accompanied by the adoption of some of the sanctions that the western countries and the like-minded have put in place. at the very least we should ensure that these third countries do not help russia to evade sanctions. by the way, the high representative was in south africa last week. from your blog, i learned what you said to them yesterday. south africa's attitude to russian aggression is being unacceptable. i hope that mr borrell has been persuasive and that the south african authorities will at least initiate a change in their position. on the other hand, we must all be very vigilant to ensure that the sanctions imposed in our countries are complied with. the united kingdom, for example, has managed to double the number of officials working in the relevant treasury agency. i hope that the european union and the member states will also strengthen these administrations.
madam president, ukraine is fighting not only for its independence, but for ours. the brutal attack of the russian federation on ukraine was only the beginning of a bloody plan to regain influence in the region. the support of the european union, the united states and nato allies has blocked the offensive, but the risk remains very high. the threat of a new russian offensive still exists. increasing the military presence on the eastern flank as well as in the black sea countries, including romania, military support for ukraine, financial support for the republic of moldova, maintaining sanctions against russia are essential for victory and possible future peace negotiations. at the same time, we need to increase european military production capacity, especially in frontline states. we need to support the population to increase the resilience of european societies, but also to rebuild ukraine on new foundations, fighting corruption and respecting the rights of all minorities. we have the strength to win this war, but to do so, european leaders must bear in mind that we can only do so by maintaining the democratic values, unity and determination that we have shown so far.
madam president, council, commissioner, almost a year ago, ukraine was brutally attacked by the russian regime. every day, every week, war crimes continue to be committed in this war of aggression. in this summit between the european union and ukraine it is essential to maintain financial support, humanitarian support, logistical support and all the support that is needed to strengthen ukraine's military capabilities. finally, the european union and nato have provided tanks to ukraine. unfortunately, we see that the portuguese government continues to hesitate with discussions between the minister of defence and the minister of foreign affairs and continues to be against the european integration of ukraine. we must all make an effort to support president zelensky and ukraine militarily, humanitarianally, financially.
madam president, colleagues, this will soon be a year when ukraine and its people will fight for the freedom, sovereignty and territorial integrity of their country. russia's unjustified military aggression has also irrevocably changed the geopolitical situation in europe as a whole. and the whole world saw the true face of the russian and kremlin regime. therefore, now is the time to focus and fundamentally take decisive political, economic and financial decisions to help ukraine fight and defeat russian aggression. let us not forget that ukraine's victory over russia is the guarantee of our security, freedom and democracy. the european union is also committed to the establishment of a permanent, independent and independent judiciary, and to the establishment of a permanent tribunal for russian war crimes, the establishment of a war victims' compensation fund and a fund for the rehabilitation of ukraine, and of course the provision of continuous, in particular military and all-round assistance to ukraine, and a firm commitment to supporting ukraine's accession to the european union.
madam president, commissioner, colleagues, it has been nearly a year that putin’s russia has waged war militarily against ukraine, but hybrid warfare against all of us – the free world, especially europe – was already ongoing for many years – also in february last year – and has been ongoing now for many years. but europe defends its citizens, its freedom and wants to achieve peace with unprecedented means and measures. this was unexpected by putin’s russia and this is why we have to underline that europe is using the european peace facility exactly for what it was meant to be used for. and europe is also fighting back in a bloodless way against the bloody war attack in terms of sanctions. yes, within europe the leadership of serbia is of concern, the leadership of hungary is of concern, but the alignment and unity are unprecedented and will remain. so it’s good to have the eu-ukraine summit this week. it’s good to talk about even more help in terms of military, in terms of sanctions, in terms of reconstruction in the future. and i have no doubt that freedom and peace will be restored.
the commission has already made a number of proposals. the war in ukraine has also put food supplies in europe and the world at large to a severe test. it is good that the commission has tried to open up new export routes from ukraine, especially while the black sea ports were closed. but the removal of all export duties and the new export routes directly to the european union have also made it difficult for farmers in the european union - particularly in the east of our union, in the countries bordering ukraine. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's agricultural products are not used for the production of foodstuffs. this, of course, puts pressure on our prices. we want to open up all possibilities for ukrainian farmers to sell their products to the european union. but on the other hand, we must not look the other way when our farmers are in trouble. we must deal with this. we must help the farmers in the european union who are suffering. but then we must, especially in the coming months and years, ensure that in a process of joining the european union, the standards of the european union are also respected in ukraine.
madam president, dear colleagues, ukraine has fought bravely against russia for almost nine years. the full—scale attack that russia launched in february last year is a clear sign that putin’s regime goal is to destroy western way of life. our task is to help ukraine as much as they need today. ukraine needs more weapons and ukraine needs a plan for recovery. the eu—ukraine summit must give ukraine a message of hope. ukraine belongs to european family. last but not least, russian and belarusian athletes should not compete on the paris olympic games next year. i am collecting your support. give your signatures to the letter i will send to the president of the european council.
i'm not going to tell you. the future of europe is being decided in ukraine. it's about whether we protect life, protect the rights of the weak and democratically decide our future, or the other side wins - disrespect for life, lawlessness and the arbitrariness of a dictator. the ukrainians are defending our space, they are defending freedom and democracy across europe, and it is our moral duty to help them and to increase not only economic, humanitarian, but also defensive military aid. it is important for the future of europe, and there has been a lot of talk about defending europe, about a european army. today, we see that we already have that army. it's the ukrainian army. we talk about the european peace project, but no one has done more for peace in europe than the ukrainians. it is therefore important that we help them on their european journey and that we also make this journey concrete at the new summit which starts tomorrow, by talking about the extension of economic cooperation, the extension of the roaming call zone or cooperation in the energy sector. such a shift in these areas will benefit not only the citizens of ukraine but also all citizens of the european union.
madam president, ukraine has already lost almost 20% of its territory to the russian aggressor. that means we should increase the eu’s support to kyiv in order to liberate the occupied territories. we should increase the financial and military assistance, at least to the level of the united states of america. after all, we are dealing with a european war. we all know that if the russians are not stopped and defeated in ukraine, they will continue towards moldova, the baltic states, maybe poland. during the past 20 years, we have been encouraging ukraine’s european ambitions without offering adequate support. our goal should be the integration of ukraine in the european union based on an unprecedented programme of economic support.
madam president, mr minister, mr commissioner, i would first of all like to thank my fellow citizens who have helped the ukrainian refugees, millions of ukrainians, since the beginning of this bloody conflict. yes, everything must be done to stop the massacre of the civilian population, because innocent citizens are dying there and i believe, commissioner, that your message today is a timid message of peace, diplomatic support. that's what we're talking about. we have to do, we have to give support to the citizens, because there the population has no more food, sometimes no more water, and we have to go to diplomatic support. but ukraine must understand, as a state that has signed an accession agreement, that it must respect the rights of minorities and that is why i ask you, commissioner, to check compliance with the statute, the agreement that ukraine has signed, because, yes, the romanian minorities cannot speak in romanian, they cannot learn in romanian and now oppressions have started against romanian orthodox priests.
madam president, commissioner hahn, on the one-year anniversary of putin's illegal, illegitimate and unjustifiable war of aggression against ukraine, this european parliament has done everything in its power to show its commitment to sanctions against russia and its solidarity with the ukrainian people suffering from this war. we have supported the confiscation of oligarchs' assets. we have supported, by means of the urgency procedure, the strengthening of eurojust for cooperation with the prosecutor's office of the international criminal court in order to prevent impunity for war crimes. and we have also supported the european union's initiative to set up a special court for the crime of aggression. but of the 11 million displaced people fleeing terror, at least 4 million have decided to stay in the european union indefinitely. and that also requires the reinforcement of european budget lines to support the solidarity that exists in the member states, to address vulnerable situations and, in particular, the schooling of minors. and believe me, it will certainly have the support of the socialist group.
member of the commission. the commission has already made a number of proposals. first of all, thank you. i think the debate has shown, again, in a big way, that there is unlimited support for ukraine and its people to win this war - i say it quite openly - i'm afraid there's no other way. i would also very much like to negotiate peace, each of us. i'm just wondering who the russian valley rand is. i haven't seen that one. i think we should also remember one thing, which has largely been lost in the debate: the issue is the restoration of ukraine's territorial integrity. sometimes i get the impression that there's a perpetrator-victim inversion in this debate. i mean, there's no doubt who the aggressor is. and that needs to be made clear, also in the future. we must do everything we can to help ukraine restore its territorial sovereignty. europe has done a great deal in this respect. if you add up the achievements of the member states, the commission, etc., military aid, humanitarian aid, that is more than the united states has done to date - just to put it in perspective. and i regret - he is no longer in the chamber - that mr verhofstadt, of all people, has had a mild populist attack here, by calling on the commission to supply arms, so to speak, knowing full well that this is not possible under our treaty, the eu treaty. but we are doing everything we can to create these opportunities, with our member states and the appropriate aid. secondly, there is, of course, a broad consensus not only in this house that russia will have to contribute in the future to the reparations, to the reconstruction of the infrastructure and many other areas of ukrainian society, and that the funds currently frozen by the russian central bank in western european and american accounts, so to speak, will also be used in the future for reparations payments, as will the funds of oligarchs. but this is a legal exercise, and anyone who has ever been involved in this matter, even in the slightest degree, knows that it is not easy. but i think the moral and political demands are clear and must be pursued accordingly. there was also the question of why we only give out loans. at the end of the day, this was a chance to help quickly. but it is also important for me to emphasize that the european member states have also agreed to finance, pay the interest on these loans for the next ten years, and the capital is not scheduled to be used until the 2030s. we shall then see how to deal with this subject. if, as we are debating today, 15 colleagues are in ukraine, at this meeting between the commission and the ukrainian government, then these are above all those colleagues who, in their sphere of competence, have topics currently open - topics open on how to help ukraine at the moment, but also on how to support ukraine's accession prospects very intensively. we do both in parallel, but, again, as has been said many times, there can be no discount for membership. but you can be very involved. we are doing that and we will continue to do so. i would like to thank you once again for the strong support which has been expressed in the debate today. i can conclude with the traditional but still important and correct slava ukrajini.
president-in-office of the council. – madam president, honourable members, commissioner, thank you for all the interventions. this exchange shows once again the european parliament’s strong support for ukraine. the eu-ukraine summit will bring a message of strong, unwavering support – a way for the eu to say ‘we stand by your side and the future for ukraine and its citizens lies within the eu’. i also listened carefully to all your interventions about accountability and further sanctions. and as i said in my introductory remarks, those responsible must be held accountable. and on sanctions, we stand ready to reinforce more restrictive measures. we will further step up the ongoing diplomatic efforts to support ukraine in all relevant international fora, calling for steadfast solidarity with ukraine and against russia’s war of aggression. we are ready to support ukraine’s initiative for a just peace, and we will continue to stand by ukraine once peace has returned – to reconstruct and to help make the future brighter for the ukrainian people. we firmly stand with ukraine for as long as it takes.
i have received five motions for resolutions at the end of the debate. the debate is closed. the vote will take place today. the commission shall be assisted by the committee of the regions.
in writing. – the upcoming eu-ukraine summit will be the first eu summit to take place in an active warzone. symbolical for itself but we need to use it. first, we need to commit ourselves to a political and financial support of ukraine until its whole territory is free and unified. this is our own interest, too. second, it’s not the time to talk but to deliver – especially military equipment. a promised equipment is nothing you can use unless it’s on ukraine’s territory. last, it is time for further sanctions towards russia and cutting it off from the international community.
a uachtaráin, the russian leadership has shown its cruelty towards the ukrainian people, engaging in war crimes as part of its standard operations on a daily basis. but not only this: they have also shown a complete indifference to their own soldiers, sending wave after wave as cannon fodder to their deaths. i voted in favour of this resolution for many reasons. i support ukraine’s independence and sovereignty, and the need to increase humanitarian aid to ukraine, and i join calls for the upcoming eu—ukraine summit to prioritise a recovery package for ukraine. however, i would like to underline my support for ireland’s longstanding policy of military neutrality. as a nation we are not politically neutral when it comes to russia’s illegal and barbaric war against ukraine. we do not oppose and would not seek to block other eu member states from providing military assistance, which is badly needed, to help ukraine defend itself. slava ukraini!
mr president, i voted in favour of this resolution with conviction because i believe that the ukrainian people need support and that all of us here should clearly state that support. it's an absolute shame, an absolute disgrace to say that the attacked should surrender and not defend themselves. it's clear who the aggressor is to all those who think and are not paid by kremlin propaganda. the aggressor in this case is russia and the russian leadership. if you want peace, you have to convince the aggressor to stop this attack and to withdraw and to seek some kind of talks from here on out, but to say that you should not help the attacked, the weaker, the one whose home is attacked, the one who is bombed, the one who is under bombs 24/7, because the war should be ended in this way, is shameful, disgraceful and is a manifestation of kremlin propaganda. this propaganda, unfortunately, continues to make its way even into this room and justify the unjustified, namely the killing of innocent people. so i voted for the resolution, and it's clear to me who is being attacked and who needs to be protected.
mr president, i naturally supported this motion for a resolution. what russia is doing is denying how it helped shape the international rules of order after world war ii. one state cannot invade another state. and these are not just questions of international law. let's look at these pictures from bucha, from irpin, from many other ukrainian cities. we are witnessing a genuine genocide, which is why i supported the amendment tabled by my colleagues from the ecr to call it that as well. these pictures show dead, dead children, kidnapped, totally decimated regions where a russian soldier's boot has entered. i am in favour of giving ukraine maximum security guarantees for the future and also supplying arms and also financial support to this state. they're defending themselves, they've been attacked.
that concludes this item.

we are here today on this historic occasion to welcome the president of ukraine, volodymyr zelenskyy, to the european parliament. this is an extraordinary moment, in extraordinary times. it has been almost one year since the brutal, illegal invasion of sovereign ukraine by russia. in all that time, mr president, your leadership has inspired your people and inspired every corner of the globe. when the world thinks of ukraine they think of heroes fighting the odds, of david beating goliath. they think of the icons of snake island, the warriors of mariupol, the liberators of so many occupied towns and villages. their names will be spoken of for generations. notwithstanding the bombs that fall every day, your grain continues to feed the world. when a devastating earthquake hit turkey and syria, you stood up and sent rescuers, equipment and expertise. that is real solidarity. (applause)i am proud to say that this house of european democracy, its members, our european union, have always stood with you. we understand that you are fighting not only for your values, but for ours. for those ideals that bind us as sisters and brothers. they make us all europeans. because ukraine is europe and your nation’s future is in the european union. (applause)we know the sacrifice that your people have endured for europe and we must honour it not only with words but with action: with the political will to ensure easier trade and with the fastest possible accession process; with funds for your people; with help in reconstruction; with training for your troops; with the military equipment and defence systems you need to win. now, states must consider, quickly, as a next step, providing long range systems and the jets that you need to protect the liberty too many have taken for granted. our response must be proportional to the threat – and the threat is existential. i grew up hearing my grandparents’ stories of my country’s last three fighter planes. when all seemed lost, when they had no ammunition, no food and no hope, when all that remained were their three barely functioning planes that they called faith, hope and charity – named after what they were fighting for, after what was at stake. the planes meant one more day of liberty, just long enough for help to arrive. they meant peace. you do not need to convince anyone here of how essential it is to support ukraine, to back all those giving their lives, to support the values we preach with concrete measures, to ensure victory, real peace – based on your 10-point plan, accountability for those who have committed war crimes and for the protection of freedom for all ukrainians. i want to repeat the promise i made to you when we met in kyiv last april: we have your back. we were with you then, we are with you now, we will be with you for as long as it takes. freedom will prevail. peace will reign. you will win. slava ukraini! (loud and sustained applause)
president of ukraine. – (the following is a transcription of the interpretation of the original speech from ukrainian into english) glory to ukraine! dear madam president – dear roberta – i thank you for these powerful words, and i thank you for your personal attention, which is very important to ukraine. i thank you for your principled and energetic approach – two qualities that we see have remained unchanged during this struggle for europe. the combination of those two qualities is a precondition for progress. europe is lucky that here, in the chamber of europe’s largest parliament – the european parliament, a principled and energetic approach commands an absolute majority. thank you, dear representatives of europe and leaders of europe. all of us europeans, each of the hundreds of millions of people in our continent, combine these two attributes, as do the representatives and leaders of europe. this combination reflects what our europe, a modern and peaceful europe, gives to the world – the european way of life, european living standards, and european rules for life. it is in europe where each and every person matters; where the rule of law exists; where states strive to be social and societies strive to be open; where diversity is a value and the values of different people are united by fairness and equality; where borders are not violated, but where you don’t notice when you are crossing borders; where people trust and have faith in tomorrow and are ready to take to the streets to fight for their future; and where there’s only one barrier between protesters and the president, and that is honest elections. this is our europe. these are rules. this is our way of life. for ukraine, it is the way home. i am here in order to defend the way home for our people, for all ukrainians of all ages and of various political convictions, various social statuses, various religious convictions and different histories. all of us enjoy this common european history – the ukrainian european way of life that they tried to annihilate through total war. why? because after the european ukrainian way of life, they want to destroy the european way of life in all 27 countries of the european union. we will not allow that. the total war that is now being waged by russia is not only for territory in this or that part of europe. the danger is that there is a dictator with massive supplies and reserves of soviet weapons and weapons from dictatorial regimes, iran in particular. in order to be able to fight like this, the kremlin, cynically and deliberately, year after year and step after step, has been trying to eliminate and destroy what we see as our values in europe. the value of human life has been destroyed in russia. for the authorities there, nobody is of value apart from those who are in the kremlin and have their own wallets at heart. the 140 million russian citizens are, for the leaders of russia, just bodies capable of carrying weapons for russia on the battlefield and in order to keep everybody obedient. the supremacy of violence and obedience are the rules instead of law. the russian regime not only hates any social justice and diversity, but they are also investing in xenophobia and trying to make it the norm on our continent. they are trying to make the inhumane realities of the 1930s and 1940s a reality today. but the answer to that for us is ‘no! no! europe!’ we are defending ourselves against the most anti—european force in the modern world. we are defending ourselves. we ukrainians are on the battlefield together with you! i thank you now for defending it together with us. we must defend ourselves. it’s important not only for the states of europe, but also for communities and societies in europe, no matter who we are or what we are. we always fight against disrespect and neglect. wherever we live, we always put our faith in integrity, for whatever we dream of for our children and grandchildren. the precondition for these dreams is peace and security. will that be possible if we do not overcome the anti-european force that is trying to steal our europe from all of us? no! only our victory will guarantee all that. each of our european values makes our victory essential. dear leaders of europe! dear madam president, ladies and gentlemen! dear members of european parliament! dear officials of the european parliament and of other european institutions here! dear journalists, security service workers, military and army personnel, police officers and rescue workers! dear workers of municipalities, diplomats, teachers, professors and scientists! dear doctors, drivers, seaport workers, farmers and industrialists! dear workers in industrial enterprises! dear owners of small and large businesses! dear workers at banks! dear energy workers and electricity grid workers! dear trade union workers! dear students and representatives of ngos! dear producers and actors, lawyers, defence lawyers and human rights defenders! the fate of europe has never depended on politicians, and there should be no such illusion now. each and every one of us matters. each and every one of you is strong. each and every one of you is capable of impacting and influencing our common outcome – our common victory! (applause) this applause is not directed to me. i’d like to thank all of you in europe – in hundreds of towns and villages – who have supported ukraine in this historic battle. thank you! (applause)i would like to thank all of you who have been helping our people, our ordinary citizens and our resettled people here, and who called on their leaders to increase and step up their support to protect our ordinary people. i thank those who were on the squares, who were raising questions, who were spreading the truth about the russian aggression, who were not tempted by russia to consume russian disinformation, and who are standing by us in our fight for life and for europe. i thank you. i thank all of you who help ukraine with vitally important supplies, weapons, ammunition, energy equipment and fuel, with the thousands of things without which we would not be able to endure this total war. i thank you, ladies and gentlemen, members of the european parliament, and you personally, roberta. with your decisions, you defend the european way of life steadfastly; you defend the ukrainian european way of life. you approve courageous decisions and you strengthen the european ambition to be a home for justice and freedom. i recall the first day of the large—scale invasion, when the russian ambition to break us and europe as a whole was still adamant. it was when the russian aggression was beginning to break its teeth fighting our defences on the sixth day of the large—scale invasion. the european parliament approved a resolution not simply to support our state, but also to approve our country’s status as a candidate for membership of the european union. that was the vision that motivated us to be strong and to stay the course. (applause) today, during the meeting of the european council, i will be able to thank personally the heads of state and government for the decisions that, over the course of the last year, have allowed our continent to do what the previous wave of leaders considered impossible. europe, at last, is relieving itself of the ruinous dependence on the russian fossil fuels. europe is cleansing itself of the corrupting influence of russian oligarchic business. europe is defending itself from infiltration by agents of russia’s secret services, who look at europe as a hunting ground for opponents of the russian regime. for the first time in its history, the european union is providing military assistance on such a large scale, and i see a positive assessment of the reforms in a european country that is fighting and defending itself while modernising and reforming its institutions. we are getting closer to the european union. ukraine is going to be a member of the european union – a victorious ukraine! ukraine is going to be a member of the european union – a victorious european union! dear friends, perhaps some of you have not felt before the power of the european way of life. now, together, ukraine and the countries of the european union have made our strength obvious. how is this possible? because we combined a principled and energetic approach. we are all equal in representing europe and integrity. we know that everybody is a leader of europe in his or her own life and specific place. we do not retreat in the face of the enemy – we defend ourselves! we are not wasting time – we are changing ourselves and we are changing things. europe will always remain europe, as long as we are together and as long as we take care of our europe and of the european way of life. i thank all of you. i invite you all to ukraine. glory to all ukrainians who are fighting, and glory to ukraine! dear colleagues, last week a devastating series of earthquakes hit türkiye and syria, causing almost unimaginable destruction. tens of thousands of people have been killed, thousands more are injured or still trapped under the rubble. i want to underline that europe stands with the people of türkiye and syria. our civil protection mechanism has been activated and help has – and continues to be –sent, and this will be debated as the first item on our agenda this afternoon. right now, our thoughts are with the families of those killed, those trapped, those injured and with all rescuers fighting the clock – night and day – to save lives. we will discuss that this afternoon – and i ask you to be present – but before that i invite you to join me for a minute of silence in honour of the many victims of this tragic earthquake. (the house rose and observed a minute’s silence) dear colleagues, 9 february was an important day for european unity. before attending the special european council meeting, the european parliament had the honour of hosting president zelenskyy – a true example of the bravery, courage and resilience of the ukrainian people. president zelenskyy spoke from our chamber, but his words really reverberated throughout europe. it was a strong message and a reminder that the battle in ukraine is not just one for territory, but also one in defence of our shared values. i want to thank you all for attending this extraordinary plenary session last week. ahead of the one—year anniversary of russia’s illegal invasion of ukraine, we have shown that the european parliament stands, and continues to stand, in full solidarity with ukraine and its people, and we will continue to do just that.

the next item is the commission statement on the eu's response to the humanitarian situation in turkey and syria following the earthquake (2023/2561 ((rsp)).
member of the commission. – madam president, honourable members, i wish to start by expressing my heartfelt condolences to the people of türkiye and of syria who have been hit by the devastating earthquake a week ago. our thoughts are with all those who have lost loved ones, the injured, those who lost their homes, and with the first responders working tirelessly to save lives. just hours after the earthquake, the european union mobilised search and rescue teams for türkiye. the european union civil protection mechanism has offered 32 search and rescue teams and 6 medical teams from 21 eu member states and 3 other european participating states. our teams arrived quickly and have participated in the rescue efforts alongside the turkish first responders since. last thursday, i visited gaziantep. the scale of destruction is without precedent in recent times. in türkiye alone, more than 13 million people have been affected. many of them are left homeless in very rough, harsh winter conditions. there is a desperate need for relief materials, including tents and other items to ensure that people have shelter and are protected from the cold. that is why i have decided to mobilise resceu – the eu’s own emergency stockpiles. we have sent 500 temporary accommodation units, 2 000 tents and 8 000 beds to turkey from these european strategic reserves. in addition, 16 member states have already offered various shelter items to help people in türkiye. we have also allocated an additional eur 3 million to humanitarian partners to meet the most urgent needs across the affected locations in türkiye. it is crucial that we also help the people of syria, who have been greatly impacted. and millions of syrians were already dependent on humanitarian aid before the earthquake, with almost 3 million displaced in northwest syria alone. following a request for assistance from the government of syria, as well as an activation of the union’s civil protection mechanism by the world food programme, nine member states so far, as well as one participating state, have already offered in-kind assistance: family tents, sleeping bags, mattresses, beds, food items, winter clothing and more. we are working to ensure that this assistance can be delivered rapidly and equitably to all people who need the assistance. the commission also allocated an additional eur 3.5 million in emergency humanitarian funding to help our partners address urgent needs in syria. they are already providing water and sanitation support, distributing blankets, hot meals, tents and hygiene items. emergency assistance is also being mobilised through a deployment of the european humanitarian response capacity. these are strategic stocks of emergency items, among others in dubai and brindisi, and they will be delivered to syrians affected by the earthquake, including winterised tents, heaters, blankets, water, sanitation, hygiene kits and kitchen sets. we will also offer logistical support to deliver assistance. honourable members, the european union is determined to further scale up support in view of this unprecedented catastrophe. considering the enormous needs, the european commission and the swedish presidency of the council will host next month the donors conference in support of the people of türkiye and syria. this is organised in coordination with the turkish authorities to mobilise international funds, notably for reconstruction and rehabilitation. i look forward to hearing your views in today’s debate.
on behalf of the ppe group. - mr president! now, a week after the devastating earthquake that shook turkey and syria, we must first of all thank those who have been fighting for the sake of those in need, risking their lives in the last week and in these minutes. respect them! it's our job to support their work in every way. let me highlight the humanitarian situation in syria. the aid started in a very difficult security and political situation. however, a much broader engagement is needed for local humanitarian actors to be able to carry out their tasks effectively. after the disaster, i contacted one of the largest aid agencies in the country, the committee for the development of the patriarchs of st. ephrem, as well as the italian humanitarian agency avsi and other non-governmental partners working in syria. the sanctions on banking transactions, electronic devices and wreck clearing machines are severely hampering humanitarian action, according to the internationally recognised organisation, which works closely with the eu. so we have to find a way to overcome the obstacles to reconstruction after the life-saving period without giving up the political goal of sanctions. this is only for the direct benefit of the syrian people! it's time! my dear officer! on this issue you can count on my personal support and that of my group, the european people's party.
on behalf of the ecr group. - madam president! - i have to go. i'm not going to let you do this. more than 35,000 dead, 2.5 million people affected by the recent earthquake, currently living in very difficult winter conditions, this is the picture from the border of turkey and syria. thousands of frozen and traumatized children, many of them orphaned in an instant. they're all waiting for us to help. i am pleased and grateful to you for launching the first european union aid mechanisms. we must rise above the borders, above the difficult political divisions, and maximize our humanitarian aid. we need to go directly to the affected areas. there are already polish organizations, both catholic and secular. they've been operating in syria for years. today, they say what they need to say. it's worth it, and i encourage you to include them in the distribution of aid. at the same time, from this place, i want to say a very big thank you to all the rescuers from all over the world. in particular, the polish group of firefighters of the husar fire brigade, as well as rescuers from the polish mining group, which has already managed to extract many people and continues to work in difficult conditions. i believe we can help many more people who are still alive. may the goodness return to you. thank you, commissioner, and let's do it! you have backup!
madam president, there are situations in which humanitarian considerations, in which the safety of the people must take precedence over any other political or diplomatic considerations. the european union keeps saying this when it comes to migrants, but it hypocritically abandons its great principles when it comes to syria. an earthquake hit it hard. we all here have thoughts for the victims and their families in turkey and syria, who are waiting in anguish. turkey is receiving support from the european union and the international community, and we welcome this. i would like to take this opportunity to greet the french rescue teams who are on the scene. but we know that the distress is even greater in syria, because of the conflict, the destruction of the country, but also the sanctions imposed by the european union. the priority now is to put an end to diplomatic rancor, to forget the blind legal logic of sanctions and to think first of all of the syrian people. our humanitarian aid must also be directed to those suffering and needy people. it's a matter of honor and humanity.
i'm not going to let you get away with this. last week's series of earthquakes struck territories that have been experiencing humanitarian disasters for years. mostly in turkey and syria. that's what makes the quick and efficient delivery of aid and search so complex and difficult. nevertheless, the institutions of the european union and the member states, including to a large extent my own country, poland, have acted very quickly and efficiently. i wanted to thank everyone for this action, but first of all, i want to say that my thoughts and prayers are with the people, the people of turkey, syria and all the other countries affected. we have to make a new effort because the scale of the destruction and the number of casualties is simply unprecedented, unprecedented in history, and previous disasters have made it impossible to measure that scale precisely. we have to be ready for long-term help.
madam president, please. the hardest part was dealing with the stories behind the pain we saw there, said the hungarian hunor rescue team who arrived home last night. our condolences to the victims of the earthquake, their families, and our hope for the recovery of the many injured. hungary was among the first to offer its help, sending search and rescue teams and medical aid to the area. the disaster relief team, together with hungarian charities, has rescued more than 30 people alive from the rubble in turkey with 167 people and 29 search dogs. thank you for your hard work and dedication. hungary is the only european state that is helping in syria. with the support of the hungary helps program, the hungarian malta charity service has deployed mobile rescue stations in the country. after saving lives, the priority now is to provide comprehensive and rapid humanitarian assistance. in addition to human tragedy, the built environment suffered tremendous damage. europe must provide all the help it can to turkey as quickly as possible, but also to the syrian region and population affected by the disaster. that's what we need most right now.
i'm not going to let you get away with this. i would like to make a special appeal for help for the victims of the earthquake in syria, where aid is hampered not only by logistical but also by political reasons. the areas where the cataclysm occurred are divided between the government in damascus and a group of rebels. this is a certificate from a medical organization working in aleppo. people are afraid of more buildings collapsing, so many families sleep outside. sometimes in cars, in public parks, on the streets, or in large fields. in some cases, social shelters and local schools have taken in displaced families. since many families share a room, there is often very little privacy, and the rooms are damp and bitterly cold. only a few have blankets or mattresses. many have to sleep directly on the icy floor. many of the shelters lack access to soap and running water, and there is still a shortage of food and blankets to keep people warm. all parties to the syrian civil war should immediately allow humanitarian aid in and allow it to be delivered where it is needed. regardless of who controls the territory.
madam president! we agree that an urgent response to the energy crisis, which is the fault of the european union's leaders, is needed. we as the ecr will vote with a rather abstention, i will propose abstention. we cannot support this proposal because the condition for our support is that the gas infrastructure investments can be financed by grants. and there's a proposal to do it differently. in the current geopolitical situation, the proposal to finance such investments with loans should simply be considered as an inefficient proposal. we are also very sceptical that repowereu will be funded by the ets. we believe that this will prevent those member states which have the money from the sale of free allowances from being able to transform their energy system. so, given that poland may have some benefits from this programme, our position is for the time being to abstain and we will speak about abstention.
in writing. so, in short, we have a power problem. it is not only the russian aggression against ukraine that is the source of this, but also our european "trunk": the naive and reckless reliance on russian gas for the green transition. polish warnings, mocked as russophobic, are now a sober assessment of the situation. repowereu should deliver on our ambitions, it should focus on rebuilding europe's supply chains of raw materials to ensure europe's energy security. and we're not going to do that without relying on a realistic balance of our energy needs. but ultimately, we have another 'ambitious' plan, based on the mirages of meeting our energy needs from green sources. in many cases, it's a cruel joke about citizens struggling with horrendous energy bills. 45% of energy from renewables by 2030? 100% growth in photovoltaics by 2025? my dear colleagues, most of you don't believe it! let's focus on finding economic solutions for cheaper gas and cheap electricity from nuclear power plants. let's make it easier for european entrepreneurs to save energy, instead of telling them how to do it! i'd like to support this project. i want a fair allocation, a real source of funding and investment in gas infrastructure. in the first case, a reasonable compromise was reached, largely thanks to the council. in the others, however, ideological inception prevailed. i call for a real plan for europe! for the safety and rational expenditure of the citizens.
the rapporteur. ladies and gentlemen! the treaty guarantees every citizen of the union who has taken advantage of an opportunity, for example, to reside, work, study or carry out research in a member state of which he is not a national, the right to participate in local elections in a member state. the directive specifying this right needs to be updated, which is why the commission has proposed amendments. i have assessed and assess positively these amendments proposed by the commission. i therefore focused on only certain modifications in my proposal for a report. at this point, i would like to thank all the co-rapporteurs for the many important amendments and the constructive work they have done on the report. first of all, i would like to mention the facilities for the disabled. i am referring to the implementation and use of assistive technologies, such as braille, large print, audio information and sign language communication. the obligation on member states to designate competent authorities to proactively inform union citizens of the conditions and details of registration as a voter or candidate is also of particular importance. i also think it is important to provide clear, specific and comparable data. the commission's commitment to establish common indicators will help to provide a better answer to the question of the efficiency and quality of the functioning of the directive's provisions in subsequent evaluations of its application. in preparing the report, i focused on ensuring that the new rules maximise equality for all citizens of the union in accordance with the treaty's legal basis. at the same time, i have ensured that these changes do not lead to additional expenditure from the budgets of the member states. i also hoped to remain within the scope of the commission's proposal to recast the directive. however, there are some changes in the opposite direction which do not allow me to support the final text of the report. one of them is the obligation to translate most of the information about the elections. the reports on the application of the directive prepared by the commission do not show that this is a factor which hinders citizens from exercising their rights. these changes will impose a significant administrative and financial burden on the municipalities. i cannot be satisfied with the desire to remove the derogation currently in force under the directive, which has been enjoyed by a dozen member states for years. i am referring to the possibility for member states to reserve the right to elect only their own nationals to certain posts. countries such as austria, belgium, the czech republic, estonia, france, greece, italy, the netherlands, poland and slovenia would have to make substantial changes to the rules to allow everyone to hold certain traditional offices, such as mayor, on their territory. the final report contains many important elements which clearly improve the conditions for citizens to exercise their rights, but it is also laden with a number of elements which i consider unacceptable. i'm not gonna be able to support him because of that.
vice-president of the commission. – madam president, honourable members, i took due note of this debate of parliament’s proposals to provide more possibilities for mobile eu citizens to exercise their electoral rights and to improve inclusiveness thereof for persons with disabilities. we are dedicated to the same goals. these proposals are only a part of the commission’s larger efforts to increase the resilience of elections and promote broad and inclusive democratic participation. there is still time to get this done ahead of the european parliament elections next year. the commission will work with the council and the presidency to find suitable solutions and achieve the unanimity required by the special legislative procedure as soon as possible.
the rapporteur. ladies and gentlemen! i want to thank you very much for all the votes and for an interesting debate. i think we can all agree that democracy is one of the values on which the union is founded and that all citizens have the right to participate in the democratic life of the union on an equal and transparent basis. i would like to stress here that the legal basis of the treaty is clear. the treaty requires that equal access to the right to vote be guaranteed under the same conditions as nationals of the member state concerned. it is about removing the barriers and inequalities that citizens encounter when they move. i believe that updating, clarifying and strengthening the existing legislation, including through the imposition of a duty to proactively inform migrant citizens about their electoral rights and obligations, is fully justified. such arrangements shall allow for broad and pluralistic participation in local elections and shall support the exercise by mobile union citizens, including persons with disabilities, of their right to participate in the democratic life of the european union. i am confident that my report, despite the doubts i mentioned at the outset, will be studied carefully and will be an important voice in the debate that the council must have. the council must reach a consensus and vote unanimously to amend the directive. i would like to thank all my colleagues once again for the opportunity to work together on the report and on the debate, and i would also like to extend a special thanks to my colleagues, especially mr marcin drwięciki for all his help.
thank you to all of you for this very, very interesting debate. the debate is closed. as you know, the vote will take place on tuesday, february 14, 2023. the commission shall be assisted by the committee of the regions.
in writing. the hungarian electoral law complies with the accessibility requirements: anyone with a communication disability due to a visual, hearing or motor disability or intellectual disability can use assistance to exercise their right to vote. political advertisements should be captioned or interpreted into sign language so that the deaf can access the necessary information, and blind people can request information in braille, but also in an easily understandable form. on the day of the vote: a braille voting template may be requested, persons with reduced mobility may vote in accessible polling stations or request a mobile ballot. a person who is unable to read or who is physically handicapped may be assisted by an assistant of his choice, or, failing that, by two members of the counting committee. the proposed amendment to the directive is welcome to move in this direction and to encourage the use of assistive technologies and formats such as braille, large print, voice-based information, tactile writing systems, easy-to-read information and sign language communication. however, the proposed amendment would also introduce new things that cannot be attributed to disability, such as advance physical voting, voting by proxy, and electronic and online voting, by referring to the needs of people with disabilities. it could be a trojan horse we don't want to let through our gates.
in writing. it is estimated that some 13.3 million eu citizens live in another eu member state than the one they came from, and more than 11 million of them are eligible to vote or stand for election. if they were nationals of one country, it would be a medium-sized member state. despite actively exercising their right to free movement and often recognising the benefits of eu membership, their participation in the elections in the countries where they live is very low compared to the citizens of the host countries. the amendment of this directive aims to remove obstacles, in particular those related to voter registration or access to information, so that eu citizens who are migrants can become more actively involved in the democratic processes in the country in which they live. it also requires member states to ensure that the needs of vulnerable and marginalised groups, such as access to voting for people with disabilities, the elderly or the homeless, are taken into account.
the epp group has sent the president a decision on the change of appointments to a delegation. this decision shall be entered in the minutes of today's meeting and shall enter into force on the day of its publication.
the next item is the debate on the commission statement on eu funding for ngos accused of corruption in recent cases and on the protection of eu financial interests (2023/2563 ((rsp)).
member of the commission. – madam president, thank you for the opportunity to discuss funding for civil society organisations today in the context of the recent revelations. the commission is politically committed and legally bound to ensure that organisations and projects involved in criminal unethical practices or those incompatible with human values don't receive eu financial support. this, of course, includes upholding the eu charter of fundamental rights and the principle of sound financial management. the commission also acknowledges and supports the key role that independent civil society organisations play for the well functioning of our democracies by upholding the common values on which the union is founded: the rule of law, fundamental rights and democracy. ngos monitor this and draw attention to threats to our values. ngos also play an important role in the detection, prevention and fight against corruption. however, there can be bad apples in the bunch, and the legal framework set by the financial regulation aims at ensuring the protection of the union financial interests. in this regard, this framework applies fully to ngos, that are subject to the same rules and the financial regulation as for any other entity receiving eu funding. prior to the award of eu funding the commission carries out rigorous selection processes, ensuring that funding is in line with eu values. during their lifetime, eu funded projects are closely monitored to ensure that they are implemented in accordance with the agreements and the description of works. the commission has not and will not hesitate to take appropriate action against any beneficiary of eu funding that is confirmed to be in breach of applicable rules. all relevant agreements establish various mechanism to safeguard the proper use of union funds. controls or audits are put in place to identify irregularities, fraud or a breach of obligations. and in the event of these occurring, including the violation of eu values, contracts may be suspended, terminated or reduced and funds may be recovered. as an example of applying the above mechanism and in view of the ongoing criminal investigation, the commission has suspended all payments for the projects in libya contracted with the ngo ‘no peace without justice’ as a precautionary measure. the commission ensures also that eu funds do not end up benefiting organisations that breach eu values, so the implementation of the early detection exclusion system. the exclusion grounds for applicants and recipients of union funds include grave professional misconduct, terrorist financing, fraud and corruption. lastly, all entities implementing eu funds also subject to eu restrictive measures stemming from article 250 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union and the connected council regulations. those who allow targeting of governments of non-eu countries, companies, groups, organisations or individuals, and prohibiting listed entities or persons from receiving eu funding. if an entity is engaged in any criminal or unethical practices or acts against the eu values, we have the legal framework in place to prevent such entity from receiving eu funding.
i am speaking on behalf of the ppe group. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. first of all, ngos are a very important part of our society and - as the commissioner has made very clear - they have vital and important tasks to perform. i am involved in several organizations myself, and so i am very aware and know firsthand how valuable the work of ngos and also of foundations is. that is why it is all the worse that some black sheep are now threatening to discredit the work and reputation of reputable organisations. the corruption scandal surrounding former vice-president kaili has shown that ngos can be used far too easily and unobserved by third countries or criminal structures as vehicles for their interests and criminal enterprises. we therefore need clear rules on transparency, funding and the activities of ngos. we need a european equivalent to the us foreign agents registration act (fara). third countries and criminal ngos must not be allowed to use them as a cover organisation to influence political decision-making in disguise, to pay bribes and to take eu funds. it has also happened on several occasions, mr commissioner, that despite all the alleged scrutiny, even organisations monitored by the constitutional guard have received financial support. we need to be more vigilant here. the financial and non-financial flows of ngos must be disclosed accordingly. i have to say, frankly, that the organizations i represent have no problem at all with doing this. this does not mean that they are disclosed in a public list, but that they are submitted for examination by the auditors and the necessary organisations. secondly, however, we must also examine whether expenditure is actually going where it should. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval. i would say that ngos must respect the eu's fundamental values and reflect this in their organisational structure. we expect the commission to do so, and to do so systematically.
the commission is therefore proposing to amend the proposal to the effect that the commission should be able to take part in the discussions. the commission has already made a number of proposals. yes, this corruption scandal must be resolved without reservation - that is, without any ifs or buts. this is true of foreign governments that have had an influence. this applies to members or former members who, as things stand, have exerted influence. and it's also true for sham ngos that have taken advantage of the good attributes of the ngo, the good attributes, the good qualities of civil society. i can say on behalf of the subcommittee i have taken over, the human rights committee, that the colleagues are determined not to allow anything to pass that would in any way discredit our work. quite the opposite. we are here for the people who have no voice, whose rights are being violated, but not for those who carry suitcases around the neighborhood. mrs hohlmeier is right: we must be careful. but let's maybe get a little more radical. it is not just ngos that are suspected of embezzling or misusing funds. all those who receive funds from us should be placed on the same bench and examined to the same extent and held to account in the same way. and we must review our practices, but not only in the committees and not only in the commission. sometimes, mrs hohlmeier, we - and this will surprise you - have to be more careful in our services, right up to the top of this house, when we grant authorisations to use the scientific services or other resources of the house without registering the relevant civil society organisations. it can't happen again. i expect a constructive debate, but one without a witch-hunt against the people in civil society that we desperately need.
