in writing. despite equal pay for equal work or work of equal value being one of the founding principles of the european union, the gender pay gap in the eu stands at around 13%. undervaluing or underpaying women's work has long-term repercussions on their quality of life, putting them at greater risk of poverty and further gaps in pension treatment. the challenge of tackling any discriminatory action based on sex, not only in relation to basic pay but also in all other related aspects, remains a challenge. strengthening pay transparency will make it easier to compare salaries of employees, help to highlight gender bias in pay and job-placement systems and improve access to justice for victims of pay discrimination. the elimination of all disparities in the workplace is a necessary step towards full gender equality.
in writing. equal pay for equal work is one of the eu's fundamental principles, enshrined in article 157 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union. the charter of fundamental rights of the european union explicitly refers to gender equality in several dimensions, including pay. despite this, the gender pay gap in the eu still stands at 13% (eurostat 2020). poland is among the five member states with a pay gap of less than 5% and equal to 4,5%. the pay transparency directive will lead to equal opportunities in workplaces. once the new rules come into effect, it will be the company's responsibility to provide the proposed salary when posting a job. regardless of the size of the enterprise, every worker will have the right to request information on his individual level of pay and the average level of pay by sex for employees doing the same or similar work as him. a directive guaranteeing equal pay for equal work for women brings a number of benefits to the economies of eu countries. improving gender equality will lead to an increase in gdp per capita of the eu of 6.19.6% and to the creation of an additional 10.5 million jobs by 2050, benefiting both women and men.
the next item on the agenda is the joint debate on the statements by the commission on the rule of law report 2022 the state of the rule of law in the european union (2022/2898(rsp)),- the rule of law in greece (2023/2628(rsp)),- the rule of law in spain (2023/2626(rsp)) and- the rule of law in malta (2023/2627(rsp)).
vice-president of the commission. – mr president, honourable members, i am pleased to be with you to discuss the 2022 edition of the commission’s rule of law report, as well as your motion for a resolution on this topic. as you know, the commission considers it particularly important to hold regular interinstitutional exchanges on rule—of—law matters. the commission’s rule of law report is being debated not only in the european parliament, but also in national parliaments, in the council, as well as in dedicated dialogues with civil society. the commission welcomes the draft resolution we are debating today on the third edition of the rule of law report of 2022. the commission appreciates that your draft resolution underlines a number of positive aspects of the report, including the four pillars it covers, namely the independence, quality and efficiency of justice systems, anti-corruption frameworks, media freedom and pluralism, and other institutional checks and balances. in follow—up to requests made by the european parliament, we also added some new topics in last year’s edition, such as public service media and the implementation of judgments of the european court of human rights. the 2022 report also covered topical issues during the year, such as the functioning of national checks and balances when it comes to concerns over the covid—19 responses by the member states and the use of spyware surveillance software such as pegasus in greece, spain, hungary, poland and france. the commission is also grateful that you recognise our efforts to engage with civil society. you also call on the commission to work closer with the fundamental rights agency. in this respect, we have indeed developed a new approach with dedicated national rule of law dialogues directly involving national civil society in a number of member states. in the preparation of the report, we are consulting stakeholders and receive many contributions from civil society organisations, professional organisations, including bar associations, practitioners and independent bodies. notable international bodies also contribute, such as the council of europe, the european network of national human rights institutions and the office of the un high commissioner for human rights. furthermore, we held hundreds of meetings with national authorities and stakeholders, including civil society. for this year’s report and in direct response to requests from parliament and civil society, we have also taken steps to further increase the transparency and inclusiveness of our work. we have prolonged the consultation period and, earlier this year, provided additional information about the country visits on our website. we now also provide more information on the composition, role and meetings of the network of national contact points on the rule of law. the most important innovation in the 2022 report, however, are the recommendations which your parliament has long called for. they are addressed to each member state. they cover positive practices and reforms which should be further pursued, as well as problems which should be addressed. these recommendations are anchored in eu law and european standards and take into account the specificity of national legal systems whenever relevant. they are tailored to the situation in each member state and are based on the clear and specific assessments contained in each country chapter. we have received positive feedback on the recommendations from you, from civil society and stakeholders, and also from the member states. we are determined to make the most of this important step forward. with regard to the findings of the 2022 report in specific member states that you added to today’s agenda and starting with malta, the report reflects that a number of reforms started in 2020 and have continued to be implemented. such reforms, in particular the reform of the system of judicial appointments, have contributed to strengthening the independence of the justice system in malta. however, challenges still remain, such as the efficiency of justice, as the length of proceedings has continued to deteriorate in recent years. several initiatives are ongoing, aimed to improve the efficiency of the justice system. in this context, the commission has recommended in the 2022 report to strengthen these efforts. although malta took additional measures and increased resources allocated to investigative and prosecution bodies, the investigation of high—level corruption cases remains lengthy. results in terms of final judgments are still lacking and remain a cause of concern. regarding greece, the report identified a number of measures aiming to improve the efficiency and the quality of the greek justice system, whilst several challenges still remain to be addressed, notably as regards the length of judicial proceedings. the commission recommended that greece address the need for involvement of the judiciary in appointments to the highest judicial offices, taking into account european standards on judicial appointments. the report also noted that the registration requirements for civil society organisations continue to raise concerns as regards its impact on the ability of ngos to operate in greece and to receive financial support. it addressed a corresponding recommendation. the challenges in the media sector, transparency of ownership, threats and attacks on journalists and adequacy of the media regulators’ resources remain concerning as well. the report recommended that the protection of journalists be improved. in relation to spain, the 2022 report highlights that the renewal of the council for the judiciary has been pending since the end of 2018, and there have been further calls to modify the appointment system in this council in line with european standards. the commission has recommended that spain proceed with the renewal of the council for the judiciary as a matter of priority and initiate, immediately after such renewal, a process in view of adapting the appointment of its judge members, taking into account european standards. spain has also increased resources for prosecution services, but the prosecution of high—level corruption remains a concern as the proceedings continue to be lengthy and complex. spain, however, continues to develop a strong integrated framework for the public administration as one means to prevent corruption and conflicts of interest. the commission has also recommended spain strengthen the statute of the prosecutor general, in particular regarding the separation of the terms of office of the prosecutor general from that of the government. to conclude, the commission is currently preparing the fourth edition of the report, which we plan to adopt in july. since february, the commission’s services have been in the very intensive phase of the country visits, meeting and engaging with three member states per week. importantly, the 2023 report will follow up and assess the implementation of the recommendations in the 2022 report.
i am speaking on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on budgets. being a citizen of the european union means something that carries weight. to be a citizen of the union means to live in a democratic system governed by the rule of law. and that's far from a given. this is something that the european union is supporting. and this is something that is consolidated and developed in the annual rule of law report that we are debating here in the european parliament today. and it is something in which the eu level is there for all citizens. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the european union is a democratic society, a democratic society based on the rule of law, based on values which are held in high esteem in the european union, such as human dignity and the right to freedom. and that is what the european union is doing for its citizens. so when the eu level asks something of the member states, it is not a centralistic wish from brussels, but to stand on the side of those who deserve and should get the rule of law systems because they are citizens of the union. the rule of law is the european union's great promise to its own citizens and to the world. investing in europe means being sure that the law is applied and that the law is spoken. that's why we're an attractive location. and that's why it's so important that we uphold the rule of law. i thank the european commission for the annual report and, in particular, the colleagues from almost all the political groups in this house for the good negotiations on the rule of law report and i look forward to a strong vote from the citizens' representation for citizens' rights with colleagues from all the member states.
the european parliament's resolution on the third report on the rule of law. i have the honour of being the author of this resolution as chairman of the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs, and the report contains some of the requests of this european parliament, country-specific recommendations, information pluralism and enforcement of judgments of supranational courts. but we ask for more. that the values of article 2 of the treaty on european union - on behalf of the ecr group. mr president, i would like to thank you for your kind words. do you want more money to raise awareness of the values of the european union? i support this position because people should know what your values really are. value number one is double standards. you want to punish poland with sanctions because it has a politicised way of choosing judges. in poland, however, judges are elected in the same way as in spain or germany. but you can go there, but not here. value number two is racism. this is the reason for the famous opinion of the venice commission (which can be seen in this resolution), according to which the same solutions exist in different countries, but there are countries which can and those which cannot, and especially those countries with a higher culture and traditions. so, germany has a higher culture than poland? that's the classic definition of racism. value number three is contempt for the citizenry. you don't care what the majority thinks, because the majority of poles have already said what they think eight times. but you don't care because you despise them and you despise their opinion. value number four censorship. so, what about the rule of law in culture? it's in europe that you censor the books of agatha christie, roald dahl, and even karl may. value number five is the inversion of the terms. well, the most important thing is, according to you, the rights of minorities, which you understand as lgbtiq and so on. but the representatives of this option in this house have long been the majority and do not respect the rights of the real minority: the minority that believes in traditional family values, the right to life or the political right to hold various offices. so double standards, racism, censorship are not european values, it is communism and you are communism.
in writing. the report once again fails to present an objective picture of the rule of law in poland. the methodology used by the european commission in its preparation is incorrect. analysis of the sources indicates a lack of differentiation and preconceived notions. the commission presents a one-sided argument, an approach that indicates the political nature of the evaluation. poland has so far actively participated in the horizontal rule of law and the separate legislative debates at the general affairs council (gac). we have indicated the need to apply the same criteria for each of the ppas in accordance with the principle of equal treatment of ppas, and to apply the appropriate methodology by the ec (distinguishing the reporting elements from the evaluation elements, diversifying the sources and not drawing conclusions on the basis of pre-established assumptions). the report should take account of all points of view without formulating political judgments. in particular, the ec cannot apply double standards. the report shall not duplicate existing eu mechanisms. in this context, we note another instrument proposed unilaterally by the ec (without consulting the ec), namely recommendations. the commission's notice on the evaluation of the implementation of the recommendations of the previous report goes beyond the review principles agreed by the eu council (council conclusions of 16 december 2014).

draftsman of the opinion of the committee on industry, research and energy. mr president, i would like to thank you for your kind words. the primary objective of cbam was to prevent emissions leakage. this is the term used to describe the relocation of companies from the european union to third countries because of the strict environmental requirements, mainly related to the price of co2 emissions. unfortunately, the legislative work in the european parliament has, as usual, turned this mechanism into a climate weapon. instead of being the regulator of the economy as it was originally intended , cbam has become another tool to punish industry for production. as rapporteur for the itre commission, i have stressed the need to maintain free ets allowances for a longer period. cbam is a new instrument and its rapid implementation is known to cause many problems for european industry. the extreme demands for the rapid withdrawal of free allowances in parliament's final text were somewhat tempered in the trilogue. yet the text is still very unrealistic. i am concerned about the higher costs for companies in the sector covered by this mechanism. these costs are ultimately borne by consumers. in addition, the cbam may disproportionately affect smaller companies and those operating in less economically developed regions, potentially leading to job losses and economic difficulties. if we do not take care of european industry, the cbam will not protect it and will remain, i fear, a naive instrument of doubtful effectiveness.
draftsman of the opinion of the committee on industry, research and energy. mr president, i would like to thank you for your kind words. i'm not going to let you get away with this. europeans have been funded for a very expensive energy transition. and i think we all realize that, especially at this moment, when there is a serious crisis caused by the war in ukraine, to rush and create a story that the sooner the better for europeans, is a mistake. because it's more expensive, first of all. the costs of this transformation will be borne by europeans. so it was necessary to create a social fund to help, to support businesses, to support households. but who's gonna pay for the fund? the europeans will pay. because while the very idea of creating such a body is acceptable, and there is a real need for it, the principles of its financing are questionable. because this fund is to be created primarily from the new financial resources of the european union itself. it is also to be created from new own resources created on the basis of the ets. what does that mean, "i'm not"? this means that those europeans who are supposed to be supported by this fund will actually be contributing to it. and now we can ask the question: is this proposal that's been put forward here a good proposal? as i say, the very idea of financial support, once the decision has been made to accelerate the transformation, is needed, but certainly not on the basis of the principles proposed here.

mr. chairman, please! the extension of the european emissions trading system to new sectors, planned for 2027, will have negative social consequences due to the increase in fuel prices and the fees associated with heating homes with fossil fuels. obviously, these records will hit countries like my country, poland, hardest, where a very large proportion of the population still heats their homes with coal or gas and where the number of electric cars in use remains small. by 2027, not everyone will be able to replace their heating with a heat pump or photovoltaics, because not everyone will be able to afford it. this will create further costs for our citizens who are already struggling with the energy crisis, high commodity prices and inflation. listening to this debate, i have the impression that not everyone realizes this, and that i, unfortunately, and my countrymen are dead.
in writing. the crisis caused by the war in ukraine has exacerbated the plight of eu citizens due to the sharp rise in energy prices. the 'fit for 55' package is also a significant burden on society, setting unrealistic targets and ambitions and having a significant impact on member states' energy mix. the negative assessment of the entire 'fit for 55' package is also justified by the fact that the vast majority of the proceedings are based on an erroneous legal basis, which creates a dangerous precedent. in this context, the initiative to establish a social climate fund as an instrument to address energy poverty should be welcomed. however, the essential objective of the establishment of the social climate fund is only to mitigate the impact of new greenhouse gas emission fees on prices that will increase as a result of the extension of the european emissions trading system (eu ets) to buildings and road transport. this scope and the direct linkage of the fund to the establishment of a new ets covering emissions from buildings and transport ets2/brt (buildings and road transport) is inappropriate. the fund must be considered as a whole, with the other elements of 'fit for 55' and the burdens it entails. this aspect should be assessed in a negative way. the new fund is needed to tackle energy poverty and the social consequences of existing eu climate policy instruments, irrespective of the ets extension.

mr president, vice—presidents, it is obvious that the eu needs a coherent strategy for its relations with china, as recent talks in beijing are simply another proof of that. even if we rightly revisit our perception of china and try to defend our policy objectives it may all be for nothing when some leaders think that they have more rights than others in setting the course of our common foreign policy. regardless of the final list of objectives, it was, is and always will be true that the union, speaking with one voice in the international area arena, may achieve a lot. divided it becomes a bystander in world politics. what should we expect of our relations with china? first and foremost, pragmatism based on a couple of simple facts. first, china is not our friend and ally who will play by our book of rules. as it believes that power is more important than business, it will not shy away from using subsidies and coercive actions, for example, to achieve its goals. we have to be ready to respond to that. second, china demands respect from the world. we should be ready to pay it, provided we make it clear to beijing that it will not come at a cost to our core values. we will also remain engaged in asian politics in order to guarantee peace and prosperity for all our partners. we should support the development of our mutual trade, but at the same time create a working roadmap on decreasing our overall reliance on china in various sectors. finally, beijing should not assume we will abandon our transatlantic relation with the us. even if washington sometimes acts like an irritating relative at the christmas table, america will always remain a part of our democratic family. what we should in turn communicate to china is that we will not become a balancer in its rivalry with the us. instead, we should declare and pursue an independent policy agenda which will ultimately only serve european interests and the global rules—based order. achieving this autonomous position will be a final stress test for the concept of eu open strategic autonomy.

we've lived through the crisis and the crypto-asset crash and we've seen clearly what practices to prevent and what we had to pay special attention to. the industry's biggest scandal, the ftx crash, was the result of a long-running misrepresentation of the true value of the assets held. our mica regulation is the answer to this problem. it shall ensure internal control mechanisms, mandatory management reporting and effective segregation of client assets from supplier assets. i believe that the core values that must be protected are the free market, fair competition and the provision of reliable information to investors to enable them to make an informed decision. equal market opportunities also mean technological neutrality, which we have ensured in this regulation. i am strongly opposed to discriminating against certain crypto assets on the grounds of environmental concerns. there is too much hypocrisy and self-interest in these efforts to believe in the good intentions and selflessness of the communities pushing for such restrictions. as the representative of poland, a non-euro area country, i am particularly pleased that the interests of these member states have also been safeguarded. taking the views of central banks into account in the licensing process or maintaining national supervision of digital money that only works in a given country is a major achievement for me as a consultant and an important element of monetary independence. in conclusion, while i have serious doubts about the usefulness and necessity of many of the acts adopted in this parliament, i am convinced today that the work done on the mica regulation will serve the people of europe well.

mr. chairman, please! cryptocurrencies are a relatively new phenomenon in the financial market and are not yet sufficiently regulated in union legislation. it is time to create a framework for the issuance and provision of services related to crypto-assets that will foster innovation and fair competition, but above all ensure a high level of consumer protection. until now, it was a gray area, a financial jungle with the promise of quick profits, but in many cases, the profits were a mirage and evaporated as quickly as they had appeared. without strict information requirements, this market will remain nothing more than a promise land for various shrewd people who exploit the naivety and low awareness of consumers. i hope that the regulation will open a new chapter and give a chance to incorporate crypto assets into the european financial market for the benefit of all citizens of the european union.
mr. chairman, please! many countries are now exploring the possibility of introducing central bank digital currencies. some of them have already done it. the european central bank has announced that it will begin testing the digital euro this year. the digital euro is expected to be secure and provide a high level of privacy, but unlike cash, it does not allow for anonymity. digital currencies have long faced public criticism, stemming from concerns that by increasing the ability to control spending, they could lead to surveillance of citizens. they will also enable new forms of social control, at a time when we are facing diminishing human rights protection, including in the european union. the concern was justified by a recording of a conversation published earlier this month, in which european central bank president christine lagarde was tricked into saying she was talking to ukrainian president volodymyr zelensky. when the fake president pointed out that europeans are protesting against digital currencies because they don't want to be controlled, the president allowed herself to be frank and admitted that the digital euro will be used to control citizens and their spending more.
mr president, commissioner, david bowie once said that tomorrow belongs to those who can hear it coming. by working on the digital euro, we prove to act on these words. the digital euro will dramatically change how our financial system functions, but it may also increase our strategic autonomy in payments, strengthen the international role of the euro, and preserve the role of public money as a monetary anchor for the payment system. however, there are many questions to be answered in order to know where we are heading. number one: what is the added value of such currency and do we really believe that our citizens need it? number two: how will the commission and the ecb guarantee privacy for the users of the digital euro without allowing it to be a great treat for money launderers and fraudsters? number three: do we have the necessary robust and resilient infrastructure to support the widespread adoption of the digital euro? without it, this project might simply become a beautiful failure.

member of the commission. – madam president, honourable members of the european parliament, since the taliban forcefully took power in afghanistan, they have structurally and systematically violated the economic, social and cultural, political and civil rights of the people of afghanistan, especially of women and girls. afghanistan is the only country in the world that prohibits education beyond primary level for girls. the taliban’s decisions on denying girls access to secondary and higher education are blatant violations of the fundamental right to education based on equal opportunity and enshrined in the universal declaration of human rights and several other international human rights treaties to which afghanistan is a state party. no religious or social reason could ever justify such unequal treatment. at a time when afghanistan is facing one of the worst economic and humanitarian crises in the world, the afghan people’s predicament is steadily made worse by the taliban, who deprive women and girls of their right to education, work and other opportunities to contribute to socio—economic life. the un estimates that since march 2022, 1.1 million girls have been deprived of secondary education as their schooling was banned. almost 4 million children are out of school in afghanistan, 60% of them girls. in certain provinces it is reported that the official curriculum is being replaced by religious education in madrassas and that forced marriages of girls have increased significantly. activists who are advocating for girls and boys education are being threatened, arrested, tortured, and many are being pushed to exile. the european union’s position is very clear. we remain committed to providing support for the afghan people as long as this can be continued in line with the eu’s principled approach, particularly the rights of women and girls. the eu will continue to call for, and exert pressure, to ensure that all afghan children – girls and boys alike – have the chance to learn. we will also continue to support afghan human rights activists and civil society as we do worldwide, reflecting a fundamental principle of the eu. this support increases raising individual cases of human rights defenders in our engagement with the de facto authorities and continuation of safe passage operations for those afghans particularly at risk. matiullah wesa is the head of penpath, a civil society organisation in afghanistan campaigning for the reopening of girls schools. he was detained on 27 march, when leaving a mosque in kabul, by a group of armed men who claim to represent the general directorate of intelligence of the taliban. his arrest was confirmed by taliban representatives on 29 march. the eu delegation in kabul has been in close contact member of the commission. – madam president, honourable members, addressing you today on the occasion of the anniversary of the schuman declaration is a good opportunity to remind ourselves of the values we stand for: a union of equality where everyone should live free from violence. i thank this house for sharing this view and for the continuous support for the common objective of eradicating violence against women and domestic violence. you have played a key role in keeping the eu’s accession to the council of europe istanbul convention high on the european political agenda for many years. the commission welcomes the positive outcome of negotiations towards the conclusion of this convention in both the council and parliament. the eu’s accession will reinforce the union’s legal framework and send a strong message to all victims of violence against women and domestic violence on the importance of women’s rights in the eu. we need to combat violence against women on a common basis and ensure all women across europe receive the same rights, support and protection. to date, protection for women at risk of violence and their access to support services vary between the different member states. the eu’s accession to the istanbul convention will play a unifying role in raising awareness and promoting the exchange of good practices throughout the eu. the eu action provides an opportunity to contribute towards international progress in ending violence against women and be a global frontrunner on this topic. the eu accession will ensure the application of all relevant provisions of the convention which fall under eu competence. we have already taken several policy and legislative measures which contribute to the implementation of the convention at eu level. we have funding in place through the citizens, equality, rights and values programme, as well as legislation on trafficking in human beings and victims’ rights, to give some examples. the commission has proposed a directive on combating violence against women and domestic violence, which aims at achieving the same objectives as the istanbul convention in the area of judicial cooperation in criminal matters. the proposed directive, once adopted by the co-legislators, will serve to implement the relevant parts of the convention on prevention, protection, access to justice, and policy coordination and cooperation. we will be putting forward recommendations on combating harmful practices. we are setting up a network of gender-based violence experts. the finalisation of the eu accession process is another step in the direction of eradicating violence against women.
the vote which will finally allow our european union to ratify the istanbul convention is certainly good news and it is a result which we, all of us here, have been seeking with conviction and determination for these years and which will finally be achieved with tomorrow's vote. the union will be better tomorrow, it will be a union which will finally recognize as a social tragedy the violence which women suffer solely because of the guilt of having been born women, solely because of the guilt of being themselves, because of their desire, because of our desire for emancipation, because of our will to self-determination. we must recognize, however, that we have come too late to ratify this. too many delays, too many crossed-over vectors have blocked a process whose sole purpose was to fight, to eradicate a disease that is very present in our societies and that is called "hatred of women". and we're getting there, we're getting there, we're getting there, but we're doing it in small steps. the steps that would be needed are very different, we would need to run, ladies and gentlemen, we would need to jump and we would need to do much more, given the numbers of this daily war that is being waged against women. i would like to join you in celebrating an important political achievement, which we have achieved thanks to the collective efforts of this parliament and of the many women who have never given up. so i would like to dedicate this symbolic, historic vote to all of them, to all of us, let me say, to the women who struggle every day, to those who do not give up despite the many difficulties and who continue to struggle, those who continue to struggle in this house but those who continue to struggle above all outside this house.
on behalf of the renew group. – madam president, commissioner, dear colleagues, finally we are making a real step to protect women and girls against violence. while there is a serious lack of data, it is estimated that 3 232 femicides have been committed over the period between 2010 and 2021 in 20 member states. femicide means the killing of a woman by an intimate partner and the death of a woman as a result of a practice that is harmful to her. one in three women have experienced physical or sexual violence. one in four young women aged 15 to 24 years who have been in a relationship will have already experienced violence by an intimate partner by the time they reach their mid-twenties. this is unacceptable. women and girls in the eu and the entire world need the safeguards from the istanbul convention. but tackling gender-based violence is not easy. simply criminalising rape, other forms of sexual violence and physical violence is not efficient. it requires a holistic approach which revolves around supporting the victim and punishing the perpetrator. the convention recognises violence against women as a violation of human rights and a serious form of discrimination against women. it obliges parties to criminalise psychological violence, stalking, physical violence, sexual violence, including rape, forced marriage, female genital mutilation, forced abortion, forced sterilisation and sexual harassment. it further obliges to invest in education, training for experts and treatment programmes for perpetrators as a part of the prevention of violence and to establish appropriate support services ensuring victim protection. these obligations are not too much to ask. this is what the women and girls need, and i am counting on this house to support the accession to the convention. and i would also like to take the opportunity to also ask the council to do right by our women and girls and ensure the swift accession.
on behalf of the verts/ale group. - madam president! - i have to go. this is one of the most important days of this term. one of the most important, because gender-based violence is the most extreme form of discrimination against women. because 33% of all european union citizens have experienced, are experiencing or will experience violence in their lives simply because they are women. the istanbul convention is for me a constitution of the rights of women experiencing violence and all victims of domestic violence. the convention is not about ideology. it's about saving lives. i thank everyone in this room who has appealed to the council and the european commission over the years. i thank the activists and the activists who have been very active in the last years in fighting for ratification. this is a very special moment for me personally. 20 years ago in poland, i worked on the first draft of the law on domestic violence. ten years ago, i coordinated the ratification of the convention by poland. and today i have the honour of being part of the european parliament's historic decision. and finally, i'd like to appeal to everyone in this room. safety, health, women's lives are not political. the violence is experienced by the voters of the ppe, the s&d, the ecr and the id. let's all stand on the women's side tomorrow.
on behalf of the ecr group. madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the need to combat violence is too serious a challenge to be used to impose any ideology. the application of concepts of the so-called gender perspective does not help to prevent violence but hinders the implementation of really effective measures. as acknowledged in its own preamble, the istanbul convention is based on gender theory, according to which violence against women must be eradicated by eliminating socially constructed roles, thus obviating the fact that the problem of violence is not due to criminal behaviour but to cultural constructions. he's moving, so it's a social issue, not a criminal one. in my country, in fact, the penalties for rapists are reduced and therefore the problem is to be tackled by promoting a change in the way we understand the relationships that confront us and generates inequality between men and women, which transforms the concept of the family as a place of conflict instead of meeting. the data show the reality: high levels of violence in countries that have based their response to violence against women on the paradigm contained in the istanbul convention. in spite of this, the european union today imposes this system, which it does not protect, by acceding to it, circumventing the objections of those member states which have not ratified the convention because they find it contrary to their constitutions. it is the member states which, in the exercise of their sovereignty, must adhere to international conventions.

the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens is a very good one. the numbers speak for themselves: one in three women in the european union has been a victim of physical and/or sexual violence since the age of 15; more than 50% of women have been sexually harassed; one in five women has been physically and/or sexually abused by a current or former partner. the council of europe convention on preventing and combating violence against women (istanbul convention) is the first legally binding instrument to prevent and combat violence against women and girls at international level. the convention is an important step towards a universal response to violence against women and girls across europe, covering prevention, protection of victims and prosecution of perpetrators through the implementation of integrated and coherent policies. as the only greek cypriot woman mep, i feel much more the weight of responsibility and the need to solve this problem. gender-based violence remains one of the greatest threats to the full enjoyment of human and fundamental rights in the world, disproportionately affecting women. we must address the problem of violence against women with holistic solutions for all women and especially for the little girls to whom we owe a future free from discrimination, intimidation and abuse.

in writing. it is a historic moment for the prevention and fight against violence against women and domestic violence. six long years after the signature of the istanbul convention, the european union is moving towards ratification. this instrument sets a fundamental objective for any civilised country that wishes to protect human rights: eliminate gender-based discrimination and achieve a future of equal opportunities for women in all sectors. nevertheless, some member states persist in delaying ratification and implementation of the convention on the basis of pretexts that hide a worrying backsliding in women's rights and gender equality. the creation of an effective and coherent european legal framework for protection against the various forms of violence and discrimination is not without obstacles. further efforts are needed to improve assistance to victims and to prosecute and punish perpetrators of crime. only together will we give a stronger european response to make women free from violence.
the next item is the debate on the statement on the situation in sudan by the vice—president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy [2023/2684(rsp)].
member of the commission,on behalf of the vice—president of the commission / high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. – madam president, honourable members, today’s debate, which i deliver on behalf of the high representative / vice—president josep borrell, is a timely opportunity to discuss the unfolding conflict in sudan, which is shaking the ground in the country and also in the region. it is an already dire situation becoming a humanitarian catastrophe. the eu strongly condemns the ongoing fighting between the rapid support forces, the rsf and the sudanese armed forces, the saf, since 15 april, in khartoum and the rest of the country. we strongly deplore the loss of lives and the violations of international law, including international humanitarian law and international human rights. so far, more than 500 people have been killed and 6 000 injured since the conflict began. these are figures in khartoum alone. the situation in the peripheries, notably in darfur, is even more dramatic and heavily under—reported. with regard to the evacuations, the majority of the estimated 1 700 eu citizens who were in sudan when the conflict erupted have now been evacuated. strong eu solidarity was at play, with eu member states organising evacuation flights, taking on board other eu nationalities as well as citizens from other countries. the eu continues to facilitate coordinated consular efforts to bring those eu citizens still in the country and willing to leave safely in cooperation with our international partners. i would like to take this opportunity to thank eu member states and their militaries for the coordinated efforts and successfully carrying out complex evacuations in sudan. we will continue pushing for a political settlement through coordinated efforts from all the international community. from day one of the conflict, the eu has mobilised its entire diplomatic apparatus. the eu’s short—term objective is to contribute to establishing a sustainable ceasefire so as to enable civilians to find safety and allow humanitarian actors to deliver life—saving assistance. we are working together with our partners to establish a broad—based coalition in this regard and following a consolidated approach. i would like to commend the efforts to increase flexible humanitarian funding for partners who are maintaining or resuming their operations in sudan. as soon as the security environment allows, we shall stand ready to step in immediately through a coordinated team europe approach. moving forward will be resource demanding, significant investment will be needed in logistics, in civil military coordination and in security analysis and information sharing, including within the un, but also between the un, ngos and the diplomatic community. beyond the attacks on civilians and on our staff, the situation is extremely worrying and requires our utmost attention. the risks of an all—out war in this country are growing exponentially. the eu welcomes the support of collective, regional and international coordinated mediation efforts, including those of the un, the african union, the intergovernmental authority on development (igad), the league of arab states, south sudan, the us and saudi arabia. they’re essential to bring sudan back to the path of peace and stability and to respect the aspirations of the people in sudan for a peaceful, stable and democratic future. close coordination to merge and consolidate mediation efforts is of key importance. the eu reaffirms its steadfast support and solidarity with the people of sudan, especially women and youth, who led a peaceful revolution three years ago. their aspirations and requests for a better future will not be forgotten. thank you very much for your attention.
on behalf of the ppe group. – madam president, thank you, commissioner, a few months ago, we were with the foreign affairs committee in sudan. it’s quite heartbreaking to see all these pictures of the recent events. the ongoing crisis in sudan has caused immense suffering for the sudanese people and threatens the stability and security of an already fragile region. it has forced more than 100 000 persons to leave the country, and the involvement of third countries has aggravated the violence and obstructed the de-escalation efforts. i call for immediate ceasefire and urge all parties to commit to a peaceful resolution to the conflict. such a resolution would enable unimpeded humanitarian access, ensuring the safety of aid workers and providing relief to those in dire need. it is vital that the european union, the african union, the un and the international community work together to find a sustainable solution and support sudan’s transition to democracy. only a civilian-led government could address the huge political, economic, security and humanitarian challenges the country is currently facing.
on behalf of the renew group. – madam president, commissioner and colleagues, first of all, i think we need to be careful in our funding, both the eu and the member states, that we focus on localisation. there are existing voluntary groups in khartoum and throughout sudan, such as resistance committees and established service committees, which are doing extraordinarily well in very, very difficult circumstances, and i think we need to make sure that humanitarian funding is actually accessible to local organisations. secondly, we have already demonstrated by our actions that we have determined that sudan is not a safe place because of the evacuations of expats. in other words, we have demonstrated that there should be a way for people to seek refuge from sudan. but there are, of course, no legal and safe pathways for anybody to leave the country. and i hope that this will be a matter for the meeting of the foreign affairs council, which takes place this weekend. it is currently not on the agenda, and i would urge the commission to try to address this. there are a number of things that we can do. obviously, humanitarian funding is one thing, and we have had so many debates about heading 6 and we need to address that very urgently. we also have to remember that the frontex has been in discussion with rsf about the externalisation of our migration policy and that is something that is come home to roost. we have also awareness that rsf has received spyware from european companies, an irish-registered company exported from cyprus and with approval from greece. so one thing we have to insist on, not to allow the status quo to return. we have to insist on making sure that we do everything we can to restore civilian authorities: the least the people of sudan deserve.
on behalf of the ecr group. – madam president, dear commissioner, today we address the alarming situation in sudan, where the ongoing conflict has resulted in the tragic loss of innumerable innocent civilians’ lives. the violence, which began on april 15th, has escalated rapidly across different parts of the country, leading to additional large-scale displacement and a dire humanitarian crisis. i deplore the fact that the parties involved have failed to engage in meaningful dialogue to resolve the crisis, leading to further destabilisation of the region. today we urge them to implement an immediate and unconditional ceasefire and allow unhindered humanitarian access. to support these efforts, the eu institutions and the member states should work with like-minded countries and organisations to access possible further steps. we must uphold our support for the aspiration of the sudanese people who desire peace, stability and democracy.
madam president, dear commissioner, dear colleagues, once again, we are witnessing what could turn out to be another humanitarian catastrophe, and it is paramount to use all our means to prevent it. however, we cannot limit ourselves to the provisions of immediate humanitarian aid. this time, it is imperative to accompany our actions with a thorough reflection on our approach to foreign policy. in my recommendation on a strategic partnership with the horn of africa and eastern africa, approved last october, this house pointed out the absolute need to establish a thorough engagement with our counterparts based on the preventive and substantial dialogue aiming at understanding the needs and working together towards the solutions. i have the feeling that this has not happened in sudan as the long-lasting problems that persist since the coup that overthrown al-bashir are at the core of the current disaster. we welcome the talks for a democratic transition, full of wishful thinking, as it should be, but we must understand that hoping for the best is not sufficient. if we do not engage constructively and in a timely, effective manner, we will always need to run for cover when it is already too late and this is something that cannot be accepted anymore.
i'm not going to let you get away with this. sudan is another country where we're basically a few steps behind russia. because we cannot have the illusion, as many commentators on political and world affairs do, that it is external influences that are the actual cause of the tensions and struggles that are taking place there. russia has been trying to gain more footholds in africa for years. sudan is basically a diamond in the kremlin's plan. a possible naval base that would block access to the suez canal and the suez canal itself, a key sea route between europe and asia. it handles most of the maritime trade between continents. if you add to that what's been dropped here, which is the issue of raw materials like gold, it basically all comes together. the european community should seek to stabilise sudan as soon as possible, although it is - i realise - very difficult to end the fighting and to expel those who are fomenting it. there is a great need for humanitarian aid, i agree, also as a diplomatic tool. and i would also like to thank the house of lords, because the increase in humanitarian aid, which will also be needed in the next budget, will be a tool that will be available.
on behalf of the ecr group. - madam president! - i have to go. first of all, i would like to make it very clear that poland, my country, is absolutely in favour of, and is a fervent supporter of, all the candidates from both the western balkans and the eastern neighbourhood in the process of enlargement of the european union. as for serbia, belgrade's strategic foreign policy directions unfortunately remain unchanged, while russia's aggression against ukraine has clearly shown that it is increasingly difficult for serbia to maintain a multi-vector policy. balancing between east and west, hiding behind the protection of its own economic interests and pro-russian social sentiments, the serbian authorities have so far not decided to join the sanctions against russia. in recent months, there has been a marked decline in the alignment of serbia's foreign policy with the eu's common foreign and security policy. the main reason for the declining degree of conformity of serbian foreign policy to around 40% is primarily belgrade's position on the russian invasion of ukraine. the lack of harmonisation of the positions of serbia and the european union was also noticeable with regard to china and, inter alia, iran and belarus. in view of the increasing divergence of serbia's foreign policy from the european union's policy, the chances of progress in the accession negotiations, including the opening of the next negotiating cluster in the first half of 2023, are slim and essentially nil. the lack of progress on the path of european integration is reflected in public attitudes. only 43% of those polled do not believe that serbia will ever become a member state of the european union. if a referendum on serbia's accession to the eu were held today, 44% of those polled would be in favour of belgrade's accession, while 42% would be against it. it should be said that serbia with putin will obviously not join the european union.

on behalf of the ecr group. - madam president! - i have to go. the minister! the european year of skills is a good idea. today, 77% of european companies have difficulty finding suitably qualified employees. we're going to need 20 million workers in the it sector by 2030, and by 2030, 60 percent of the adult population should be in training, compared to 37 percent today, so it's an incredibly ambitious task. at the request of my political group, the employment committee is organising a public hearing on the diseases of the labour market and the professions of the future. that's an important topic too. the importance of digital skills and the risk of a digital divide were also mentioned in our proposal. in addition, the requirements of the reference to the promotion of vocational training and events such as euroskills, which help to show the attractiveness of the career path, are taken into account. this is important because this year's edition of the event, which promotes the world's champions of the sport, will be held in poland. the negotiations with the council also succeeded in rejecting the problematic provisions proposed by parliament on the legal framework for paid traineeships. my group will support this proposal. there is no doubt that we all need to know that lifelong learning, lifelong learning, will soon become a duty for all of us.
madam president! the modern labour market offers citizens enormous opportunities, but also presents specific challenges. lifelong learning and continuous upskilling are now a natural part of the european economy, which requires highly skilled workers. i speak with pride as a representative of poland, a country that is one of the three most competitive markets for the it industry in central and eastern europe. we are also the second most attractive market in the world for it outsourcing. but we have to realize that there is a serious innovation gap compared to the global market. i therefore call on the european commission to step up its efforts to ensure that workers' skills are properly matched to labour market needs, so as to enable citizens to fulfil their professional potential and to increase the competitiveness of european businesses, which is something we all need to be concerned about. and let's not forget the elderly. it is necessary for the union to take steps to protect older people from digital exclusion. that's the only way we're going to build a truly equal society.

the next item is the report by mrs biljana borzan, drawn up on behalf of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, on the proposal for a directive of the european parliament and of the council amending directives 2005/29/ec and 2011/83/eu as regards the empowerment of consumers in the ecological transition through better information and better protection against unfair practices (com ((2022) 0143 - c9-0128/2022 - 2022/0092cod)).
on behalf of the ecr group. - madam president! - i have to go. ladies and gentlemen! many of you have probably made a decision to buy a product based on the label 'green' or 'environmentally friendly'. more and more citizens are doing so. unfortunately, it's often just pseudo-ecological marketing that has nothing to do with real environmental assessment. the report aims to curb this kind of practice, which misleads consumers. i would like to thank the rapporteur for working with me on this project. i think we have succeeded in producing a balanced text which is beneficial to consumers but at the same time does not impose disproportionate burdens on businesses. my priority is to treat consumers equally and fairly, as is reflected in the amendments i have tabled which have been compromised. and thank you for that, too. they will ensure that citizens receive the same conditions and duration of the commercial guarantee for the same product in all member states. i strongly reject the amendments to the vote put forward by the greens and the left which prohibit the promotion of products derived from fossil fuels. they are vague and de facto prohibit the advertising of products for everyday use, but also those needed for the energy transition, such as solar panels. these solutions are certainly not designed to protect the environment, but are merely a sign of green populism. there's no approval for these changes, and my group will not support them.

in writing. the european commission's reckless decisions and disastrous sanctions policy have left citizens and businesses facing a serious energy crisis. energy markets have been hit by hectic price fluctuations, which have raised recession accounts across europe and undermined investment confidence. three lessons can be drawn from this situation. first, it has been proven that sufficient and affordable energy is vital to the functioning of the european economy. renewable energy sources therefore also play a key role in achieving the union's energy independence. increasing their share in the energy mix should be supported from both security of supply and climate protection perspectives. on the other hand, it has become clear that energy policy problems generated by the commission can be solved by the member states. the achievements so far have been made possible by the sacrifices of citizens and the swift and decisive action of governments. a good example of this is hungary, which has implemented one of the fastest solar energy programmes in europe in recent years, while maintaining the protection of the grid. finally, it became clear that the proposals in brussels were more likely to hinder efforts to increase the use of renewable energy. the impact assessment of the directive, which was carried out before the energy crisis, is outdated and the objectives set are therefore unfounded. furthermore, the commission is also financially hindering renewable energy projects by blocking eu funds to hungary and poland. this is unacceptable the commission should abolish the harmful sanctions, member states should hand over their resources and should provide all the help they can to promote investments in renewable energy.
the next item is the debate on the report by ms kateřina konečná, on behalf of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, on the proposal for a directive of the european parliament and of the council on consumer credit (com (2021) 0347 - c9-0244/2021 - 2021/0171 (cod)) (a9-0212/2022).
the rapporteur. mr president, it is not easy to take over such a large and important legislative document as the rapporteur of the smallest political group. however, i believe that given the context of the current economic crisis, as rapporteur for the left, i have stood up and prepared legislation that will significantly protect consumers who take out credit. before i get to the actual analysis of what went well and what didn't, let me thank all the shadow reporters, their assistants and political advisors, including the secretariats and linguists. without you, this work would not have been possible. i cannot forget to thank the two presidencies which took part in the new directive, the czech and the swedish. last but not least, i'd like to thank the commissioner and his amazing team. it's been a long and difficult journey. i'm not going to lie, at the end of it is a result that is definitely not perfect and in every way not what i would have wanted as a left-wing politician, but it is one that i can stand behind with pride. and i think the biggest achievement of my vision is that we have, for the first time, managed to enforce what's called the right to be forgotten for cancer patients in european union law. for years, there have been all sorts of patient forums talking about the discrimination that cancer survivors experience on a daily basis in the financial markets. in future, those who have recovered from cancer and apply for a loan for which insurance is required will be protected by a 'right to be forgotten' guaranteeing that, after a certain period of time has elapsed since their treatment ended, a bank or insurance company will not be able to use their cancer as a reason to undermine the credit insurance. personally, i would like to see this right extended to all major diseases, not just cancer, but the member states of the european union have agreed only on cancer. anyway, the door opened. once a law is established, it may gradually be written everywhere. another thing i'm particularly proud of is the new credit advertising regulations. like cigarette advertising, new loan advertising should always include a clear and strong warning that borrowing money simply costs money. at the same time, certain dangerous and misleading types of advertising will be prohibited. personally, i think that consumer credit advertising should be banned completely, at least in the mass media, but unfortunately, that has proven to be completely impractical. overall, the new directive covers a number of financial products that have not been regulated so far, such as credit cards and various micro-loans. i am particularly pleased that, after a hard fight with the council, the directive has been extended to the now widespread 'buy today pay tomorrow' schemes. new regulation is needed especially against big tech companies, as they are assertively trying to enter the credit market and wield enormous power to drive consumers to impulse purchase. i consider the biggest failure of the negotiations to be our failure to enforce the obligation on member states to set a higher maximum chargeable interest rate for consumer credit or, alternatively, a maximum price for a credit that the borrower is obliged to pay for the credit. in my view, this would be by far the most effective consumer protection tool. colleagues, once again, i want to thank all of you for your smooth cooperation. i look forward to the debate and strongly hope that the document will pass the final vote smoothly and that the new consumer credit directive will then be swiftly implemented by the member states as well. in my opinion, it was too late yesterday and we can't afford to waste any more time.
member of the commission. – mr president, honourable members, i am pleased to be with you today for this debate about the new consumer credit directive. i am replacing commissioner reynders, who unfortunately could not be here this evening. the 2008 consumer credit directive has proven to be an essential tool to protect eu consumers taking out credit. however, after 15 years, it was necessary to update it. indeed, the digitalisation process profoundly changed the financial sector and new forms of credit are being proposed to vulnerable consumers online. the current cost of living crisis is also putting many european consumers under strain. many need to borrow for their basic needs and we must ensure they are properly informed about the risks of taking out credit. in this context, the commission welcomes the agreement reached by the co-legislators in spring and is very happy that the text is now ready for a formal final adoption by parliament. the commission believes that the new rules will improve the protection of consumers and will foster responsible practices both online and offline, thus increasing consumer confidence. they will also create a clearer framework for credit providers. i would like to thank you for the work done by parliament and in particular by the rapporteur, ms kateřina konečná, and the imco committee on this very important proposal for consumer protection. we particularly welcome that the co-legislators found an agreement on several important improvements of the consumer credit legislation. let me highlight a few of them. first, the scope will be extended to reflect market developments. new products such as ‘buy now, pay later’ schemes, as well as often risky small amount high-cost loans will now be covered by the rules. we also appreciate that pre-contractual information disclosure online will be improved. this will empower consumers to make more informed choices and effectively compare offers with online tools. moreover, a warning about the costs of credit for all advertisements will increase consumer awareness. the commission welcomes the further strengthening of the proposal regarding creditworthiness assessment – one of the key provisions of the directive. new rules will make sure that only relevant data are used for such assessment. moreover, credit will only be granted to consumers who are deemed able to repay their debt sustainably. the new provisions on measures to cap the cost of credit on forbearance and on debt advice will prevent the offering of excessively expensive credits by design and strengthen over indebtedness prevention. they will ensure that consumers in difficulty are adequately supported and do not fall in debt spirals. the agreement also provides for effective and dissuasive penalties in line with horizontal consumer legislation. finally, it is positive to see that the new directive establishes rules for adequate admission, registration and supervision of all credit providers. once the text is adopted, the commission will work closely with member states to ensure a smooth transposition of the new rules. the commission strongly believes the new directive is a major step forward to protect consumers against risky credit products and over indebtedness and to create a more harmonised framework for businesses offering credit. we are very proud of the results achieved.
rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on economic and monetary affairs. – mr president, consumer credits may be a source of hope and happiness, but they can also lead to personal drama and tragedies for whole families. this is why i welcome the deal on the consumer credit directive. as econ rapporteur on the file, i fought for this document to enable people to make informed choices when taking credit. i welcome that, according to the new rules, a negative creditworthiness assessment precludes a credit agreement from being concluded. the new rules correctly oblige the lender to make sure that consumers have easy access to all necessary information, including the total cost of credit. moreover, knowing how many families fall into that spiral, i welcome the fact that the directive encompasses also small loans below eur 200. if well implemented, this directive lowers the risk of consumer credits being a source of personal tragedies. in a world of many doubts, this security is to be welcomed.
member of the commission. mr president, madam rapporteur, dear marion wallsman, ladies and gentlemen, on behalf of the european commission, i would first like to congratulate the european parliament and the council on the impressive work they have done over the last 18 months on the proposal for a regulation on the protection of geographical indications for handicrafts and industrial products. this is the last straight line. we are now on the threshold of a new system of geographical indications, a safer, more visible system, at the service of producers and consumers, but also, of course, of our european regions, all of whom are working to protect our industrial, but also cultural, european heritage. this collective effort around geographical indications is a reminder of the importance of intellectual property as a key driver of economic growth in an increasingly intangible economy. let me remind you that ip-intensive industries already account for 45% of the total gross domestic product of the union, but also for 93% of the european union's exports. the regulation we are discussing today is one of the flagship initiatives of the ip action plan 2020. it is intended to strengthen the competitiveness of industry, and of course of smes, throughout the union, but also, it must be said, to fill legal gaps. for more than thirty years, only agricultural products were concerned, and with this proposal, that will no longer be the case. the regulation also fills a legal gap at international level. producers of industrial and craft products will be able to apply for international protection of their geographical indications under the geneva act, and thus benefit from the protection granted by the european union's international agreements which, as you know, currently only cover products with a single agricultural geographical indication. during the trilogues and thanks in particular to parliament's amendments, the text was strengthened and i really wanted to thank you for this, madam rapporteur, as well as, of course, all the fictitious rapporteurs who have worked towards this, to have a very constructive approach. it is therefore an ambitious text, which fully recognises the importance of geographical indications. i would like to conclude, if i may, by saying a few words about smes, which will be the main beneficiaries of this regulation, and i am grateful to the european parliament for its advocacy on their behalf. several provisions are specifically aimed at these companies to encourage them to register their geographical indications in the european union. the aim, of course, is to reduce the administrative and financial burden of the registration procedure for smes, with the possibility of benefiting from the technical assistance provided by the member states' authorities for the preparation of their application. this regulation is one of the many initiatives of our commission in favour of smes, a major priority in the light of the major challenges facing smes today. we shall return to this in the sme package, the famous package of measures to support smes, which, i would remind you, must be adopted tomorrow in committee. i know that the european parliament is fully aware of the challenges of the new geographical indications system and i am confident that you will support this new regulation.
rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on international trade. – mr president, the establishment of the eu-wide protection system for geographical indications for craft and industrial products is a long-awaited initiative. building on the years of successful promotion of gis in the food and spirits sector, we may now use similar tools to protect and promote european craft and artisanal products. with the introduction of the eu-wide registration system, which guarantees uniform laws and protection for eu producers, we should not forget about the establishment of proper counterfeiting mechanisms and active controls of e-commerce trade. it is now the role of dg trade to extend the protection to third countries by the means of revised trade and gi protection agreements. it is my hope that with simplified registration procedures and support of eu and local authorities, our producers will benefit from increased protection and promotion of their products within the common market and beyond.

on behalf of the s&d group, madam president, the socialist group has fought for many years for a real change in the eu's trade policy and it is right to say that today we have finally succeeded. the free trade agreement with new zealand is the most ambitious and progressive trade agreement ever concluded by the union. and above all for one reason: this is the first agreement to impose sanctions on non-compliance with commitments to sustainable development, namely the paris agreement and the international labour organization conventions. the sustainable development chapters are thus no longer the poor relative in trade agreements. new zealand is an important strategic partner and this agreement is worth more than the volume of trade involved. a massive majority support from this parliament will help to make the eu-new zealand agreement a reference point for the future of trade agreements, for new generation trade agreements truly worthy of that name.

madam president, ladies and gentlemen, dear colleagues, this free trade agreement negotiated over the course of four years and signed on 9 july indeed marks a significant milestone in our bilateral relations. it reinforces new zealand aotearoa as a valued trade and investment partner and it strengthens the european union’s presence in the indo-pacific. all in all, as many colleagues have mentioned, this agreement removes trade barriers for companies on both sides, particularly when it comes to tariffs, public procurement and the protection of intellectual property. this promises legal certainty and, hence, ultimately incentives, investment and trade. yet this agreement is not merely a trade and investment deal. it is a testament to shared values and mutual trust between two like-minded partners. just like us, new zealand adheres to the paris climate agreement, new zealand is committed to sustainable development and new zealand is dedicated to core labour standards. in a world facing increasing protectionism, new zealand and the european union have together set a positive example and laid an important foundation for fair, rules-based and sustainable trade. i am glad that all our member states have understood just how important new zealand aotearoa is in this process, and i trust that we will pave the way for the agreement to come into force in the first half of next year.
madam president, with increasing geopolitical risks and reshuffling of global value chains, the eu needs to maintain competitiveness, promote international cooperation, and increase our strategic autonomy. therefore, we warmly welcome the recent eu-new zealand fta. its ratification will send a clear message that we are open and willing to work with partners across the world. however, this cooperation must not only mean strengthening our respective economies, but also addressing common challenges, like those related to sustainability and climate change. with the most progressive text ever adopted in the fta by the european union, with its strong tsd chapter, a broad set of mutually agreed commitments, and a sanctions-based enforcement mechanism, the deal with new zealand should be seen as an ambitious but achievable paradigm for a new generation of trade agreements.

it's in writing. the report contains extremely dangerous proposals for amendments to the treaty on european union and the treaty on the functioning of the european union. these proposals are unprecedented. they represent the handing over of power to eu officials and the dictatorship of the largest countries, and de facto germany, at the expense of the middle and smaller countries. they aim to transform the union from a community of sovereign nation states into a centralised european superstate based not on the principles of cooperation and consensus but on coercion. this is not a federation plan at all. the project envisages the creation of two new eu exclusive competencies in the fields of environment and biodiversity, as well as a significant expansion of shared competencies to seven new areas: foreign and security policy, border security, public health, civil defence, industry and education. the proposal calls for a withdrawal from the 65-area unanimity voting system, which means the near complete abolition of the last bastion of sovereignty, the veto. the euro is to become the currency of choice for the member states. the amendments are purely political and ideological and cannot be found in a constitutional document. among them, for example, the substitution of the term "gender equality" in the treaties.
